Spec 002  ·  NinjaTrader Trade Copier Add-On  ·  2026-07-15
Prop-Firm Trade Copier
NinjaTrader Add-On. One window, no chart dependency. Watches all instruments simultaneously. Rules-based: each rule maps one instrument + master account to a follower account set. ATM manages brackets. Broker-side resilient.
B1–B15 COMPLETE · B16 in progress ~5300 lines · 5 files · 99 tests ATM-native brackets Add-On + ChartTrader panel
Overview
The problem today
SimpleTradeCopier copies limit orders after fill — worse fills on followers. It is chart-bound — one chart = one instrument. Trade MES + MNQ simultaneously and you need two charts open, two instances configured, both staying active. One closes accidentally and that instrument stops copying silently.
The solution
A NinjaTrader Add-On — a standalone window with no chart dependency. One instance watches all instruments. Rules-based: each rule binds one instrument + master account to a follower set. ATM manages all brackets natively per account.
What ships in Phase 1 (Block 1)
The smallest perfect thing - Add-On window (rule management + status) + ChartTrader panel (where you trade: copy toggle + trim + break-even button) + shared CopyEngine. One rule, 1 instrument, multiple followers (ListBox multi-select). ~1401 lines across 4 files. Each subsequent block is additive.
NT Owns / We Own — the parasitic infrastructure split
Core principle: We do not build a tool that sits on top of NinjaTrader. We extend NinjaTrader — using its infrastructure as if it were ours — and bolt modular features onto the seams it already exposes. If NT already owns it, we never touch it. Our total footprint is one event hook, one gate chain, and two injected UI rows.
The ownership split — every layer accounted for
Layer NT's Infrastructure (NT owns — we borrow) Our Code (we own — the only thing we write)
Account management Account.All — discovers, connects, and maintains every broker account. Works for Apex, TopStep, Rithmic, any broker NT supports. Zero. We read Account.All in one line.
Order state events Account.All.OrderUpdate — a single event that fires for every order state change on every connected account, all instruments simultaneously. One subscription. One handler. That handler IS our entire copy engine.
Order routing NT routes CreateOrder() + Submit() to the correct broker API. Rithmic, CQG, IQFX — NT owns all that plumbing. We call account.CreateOrder(). One line. NT handles everything after.
Bracket management ATM (Automated Trade Management) — stops, targets, breakeven, partial exits, trails. Submits atomically with entry. Runs broker-side. Survives NT freeze. Zero. We never write a stop or target line. User selects ATM template in ChartTrader before placing entries.
ChartTrader window NT renders and manages the ChartTrader panel — the entire Buy/Sell/Close UI, account selector, ATM selector, quantity box. We inject one row at the bottom of ChartTrader's existing row stack. NT owns the window. We own one row inside it.
Add-On window frame NT's NTWindow class provides the window chrome, docking, menu registration in Control Center, persistence. We subclass NTWindow and fill the content area. NT owns the shell. We own the content.
UI theme & styling NT's WPF resource dictionary — NTButtonStyle, AccountComboBoxStyle, NTBrushes — every color, font, border, hover state. Zero custom styles. We reference NT's own keys. Every control inherits NT's theme automatically.
Position tracking account.Positions — live position quantity per account per instrument, updated by NT on every fill. Trim reads account.Positions[instrument].Quantity. One read. NT owns the number.
P&L tracking account.Get(AccountItem.RealizedProfitLoss, Currency.UsDollar) — daily P&L per account, maintained by NT. P&L cap gate reads this one value. NT owns the calculation.
Hotkey registration NT's keyboard hook infrastructure — KeyBinding in NT's WPF input system. We register one KeyBinding for Shift+T. NT fires it. We handle it.
What we actually own — the complete inventory
── The only seam we own ────────────────────────────────────

CopyEngine.cs (~150 lines)
  OnOrderUpdate()   ← the ONE hook into NT's infrastructure
  Gate chain        ← enabled? source? submitted? dedup? P&L?
  SendCopy()        ← calls account.CreateOrder() — then gets out
  Trim()            ← reads position, computes ceil(n/2), submits
  IsDedup()         ← ConcurrentDictionary TTL, no lock()

TradeCopierPanel.cs (~90 lines)
  One injected row  ← sits inside NT's ChartTrader row stack
  Toggle button     ← calls CopyEngine.SetEnabled(bool)
  Trim button       ← calls CopyEngine.Trim(instrument)

TradeCopierWindow.cs (~80 lines)
  Content area      ← inside NT's NTWindow shell
  Rule display      ← per-instrument rows with NT-native controls
  Status log        ← CopyEngine events → TextBlock lines

─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
Total: ~770 lines (B1 actual). Everything else is NT's.
Zero-Launch UX (Pillar 5)
The user never "starts the copier." They open NinjaTrader. The Add-On is already registered in the Control Center menu. The ChartTrader panel row appears automatically when a chart opens. The OnOrderUpdate hook is live the moment NT connects accounts. There is no startup moment. NT now has a new capability — copy and trim — exactly as if it shipped that way.
The decision gate for every future feature
Before writing any new code, ask: "Does NT already own this?"
If yes — read the NT API, call it in one line, move on.
If no — add it to the gate chain or as a new injected row.
Never replicate what NT already provides.
Problem Statement
SimpleTradeCopier — what breaks
ScenarioSimpleTradeCopier behaviourImpact
You place a limit order on master Waits for OrderState.Filled, then sends a Market order to followers ✗ Followers chase price. Worse fill guaranteed.
Market moves away after your fill Followers still get market order at current price ✗ Slippage multiplied across accounts
Duplicate order event fires No dedup guard — sends twice ✗ Double position on follower accounts
Account already at daily loss limit No P&L cap — copies regardless ✗ Blows prop firm daily drawdown rule
ATM bracket orders (stop, target) Only entry is copied — bracket legs ignored ⚠ Must manually manage brackets on followers
V12 SIMA — right answers, wrong moment
V12 solves every problem above and more. It is not being discarded — it is being archived as Phase 2–4 building blocks. The reason it is not Phase 1 is scope-to-usage mismatch:

~380 KB
Code needed for Phase 1
~15 KB
Trades per day (current)
1–2
Photon ring buffer, FSM per follower, REAPER orphan detection — all correct architecture for high-frequency multi-exit strategies. Plug back in when the trading style demands it.

Key Design Decisions
COPY WHEN
OrderState.Submitted for market orders. OrderState.Submitted for limit orders (same price, live on exchange simultaneously). Not on fill — that is the SimpleTradeCopier mistake.
BRACKETS
Delegated entirely to NinjaTrader ATM. Set up one ATM strategy, select it in ChartTrader before placing entries. NT attaches an independent ATM instance to every copied order on every account. Trailing, stops, targets — all run native.
DEDUP
10-second TTL keyed on orderId (NT8 order IDs are unique per order event). Prevents double-fire on NT's duplicate OrderUpdate events (known NT 8 behaviour).
ACCOUNTS
Prop-firm agnostic. Per-instrument independent selection. Every rule has its own leader and its own follower set — MES leader ≠ MNQ leader unless you configure them that way. No hardcoded broker prefixes. Works with Apex, TopStep, Earn2Trade, FTMO, or any broker connected to NT8.
P&L CAP
Optional daily P&L floor per account. If EnableDailyLossCap = true and account realized P&L is below the configured threshold, that account is skipped for the dispatch. Prevents copying into a blown account.
HOST
Two native NT surfaces, one shared engine.
ChartTrader panel — primary. Copy toggle + Trim button live right next to Buy/Sell where you already look. One panel per chart, appears automatically when ChartTrader is open.
Add-On window — secondary. Rule management, status log, global on/off. Open from NT Control Center menu. Stays open independently of charts.
Both surfaces call the same CopyEngine singleton — toggle ON from either surface and both reflect it immediately.
RULES
Each rule = one instrument + one leader + one follower set. Rules are independent — MES copies to Acc-A,B,C while MNQ copies to Acc-D,E,F.
Account selection UI: Leader uses the standard NT ChartTrader account ComboBox — the exact same control NT already shows. Followers use a matching NT-styled dropdown that opens to a checklist of all accounts with a checkmark per follower. Both controls use NT's own WPF theme — indistinguishable from native NT UI.
🔥 HOT POTATO
Architectural pillar: do the minimum, pass responsibility immediately. We never own what NinjaTrader or the broker can own natively.
Brackets → ATM. Trailing → ATM. Order routing → broker. UI state → NT ChartTrader. Trim qty → one line math. Copy timing → on Submitted, pass and forget.
The copier is a relay. Receive signal, forward signal, get out of the way. The system that does the least breaks the least.
SCOPE
Building-blocks: smallest perfect thing first. Block 1 = ChartTrader panel + Add-On window + shared CopyEngine (~320 lines, 3 files). Every subsequent block is additive — zero rewrites. Jane Street JS-010 (private constructors), JS-021 (no lock), JS-023 (volatile bool), JS-025 (ConcurrentDictionary dedup) enforced throughout.
⚡ ZERO-LAUNCH
Pillar 5: The product has no startup moment visible to the user.
The user opens NinjaTrader. The Add-On is already in the Control Center menu — registered at install, not at runtime. The ChartTrader panel row appears automatically when any chart opens ChartTrader. The OnOrderUpdate hook is live the moment NT connects accounts.
There is no "run the copier" step. No separate process. No splash screen. NT now has a new native capability — copy and trim — as if it shipped that way from NinjaTrader themselves. Users do not feel like they installed a third-party tool. They feel like they unlocked a feature that was always there.
🗺 LIVE MAP
Pillar 6: Every pixel on every surface is a function of live system state — not of user action history.
The ChartTrader Panel and the Trade Copier Window are not two UIs. They are two viewports into the same live system.
Four layers:
Layer 1 — Labels are state: Control text is always the live value. "2 selected", "Copy ON", "MES: 4 long | +$120" — never a placeholder, never a prompt.
Layer 2 — Color is state: Green = active/safe. Amber = caution/partial. Red = destructive available. Dark grey = action not currently meaningful. Text green = positive P&L. Text red = negative P&L. Color is never decorative.
Layer 3 — Enabled state is state: A grey button is information. It means: the system state this action would operate on does not currently exist. No position open = Flatten is grey. No working entries = Cancel is grey. Evaluated live on every PositionStateChanged event from CopyEngine.
Layer 4 — One system, any surface: Toggle Copy ON from the Panel — the Window goes green simultaneously. Position opens — both surfaces color simultaneously. CopyEngine fires PositionStateChanged(instr, hasPos, hasEntries). All surfaces subscribe. No surface holds UI state that can drift from reality.
Full spec: docs/standards/PTT_DESIGN_PILLAR.md — color constants, event pattern, NT8 scan compliance, per-button state gates.
Trim Feature — Shift+T — Sell Half Open Contracts
Jane Street Correctness by Construction (JS-003 / JS-016): The TrimSignal struct carries no quantity field. It is structurally impossible to accidentally sync contract counts across accounts — each account reads its own live position and computes its own half independently. Quantity synchronisation is an illegal state that is unrepresentable in the type system.
Decisions
QuestionDecisionRationale
Which accounts get trimmed? Master + all followers for that instrument One action trims all accounts in the rule simultaneously. Never trims accounts belonging to a different instrument's rule — MES trim never touches MNQ accounts.
Which instrument? One instrument at a time — always Trim is scoped to the instrument of the chart you are on (ChartTrader panel) or the rule you select (Add-On window). Two instruments are never trimmed simultaneously. This is enforced structurally — TrimSignal.Instrument is a required field, not optional.
How is "half" calculated? ⌈qty / 2⌉ — ceiling Odd qty (3 contracts) → trim 2, keep 1. More aggressive than floor — reduces exposure faster.
Are quantities synced across accounts? Never — each account is independent Follower A has 2, Follower B has 1 (partial fill). A trims 1, B trims 1. They do not mirror the master's qty. Correctness by construction — TrimSignal has no qty field.
What if an account is flat? Skip silently If position qty == 0, no order is sent. Log it in the Add-On status panel.
Order type for trim? Market order Trim is an exit. Speed matters more than price. Market order guarantees execution.
Trim signal flow — Shift+T pressed
flowchart TD HK([Shift+T pressed]) --> AW{Add-On window\nfocused or\nglobal hotkey?} AW -- Global --> AR{Active rule\nhas instrument?} AR -- No --> ZR([Log: no active rule]) AR -- Yes --> LOOP[For each account\nin active rule] LOOP --> POS{Get live\nposition qty} POS -- Flat / 0 --> ZF([Skip — log flat]) POS -- Long N --> CALC[trim = ceil N/2\nSell Market trim qty] POS -- Short N --> CALC2[trim = ceil N/2\nBuy Market trim qty] CALC --> SEND([Submit — broker side]) CALC2 --> SEND style HK fill:#111520,stroke:#a855f7,color:#a855f7 style SEND fill:#111520,stroke:#22c55e,color:#22c55e style ZR fill:#111520,stroke:#1c2133,color:#4b5563 style ZF fill:#111520,stroke:#1c2133,color:#4b5563 style CALC fill:#111520,stroke:#f59e0b,color:#f59e0b style CALC2 fill:#111520,stroke:#f59e0b,color:#f59e0b
TrimSignal struct — correctness by construction
❌ Wrong — quantity in signal (illegal state possible)
// BAD: quantity in signal
// Could accidentally sync all accounts
// to master's qty — violates JS-003
struct TrimSignal
{
  public OrderAction Direction;
  public int Quantity;  // ← BANNED
  public DateTime Timestamp;
}
✓ Correct — no quantity (illegal state unrepresentable)
// GOOD: signal carries timing only
// Each account reads its OWN position
// qty sync is structurally impossible
private readonly struct TrimSignal
{
  // NO quantity field — by design
  public readonly DateTime UtcTime;
  public readonly string   Instrument;
  // Each account: ceil(own_qty / 2)
}

The Add-On Trim button is per rule row — clicking [TRIM ½] on the MES row trims MES accounts only. Clicking it on the MNQ row trims MNQ accounts only. Two instruments are never trimmed simultaneously by any surface. Shift+T always resolves to the instrument of your currently focused chart — if no chart is focused, it resolves to the first active rule.

NinjaTrader Freeze Resilience
Key insight: NinjaTrader's GUI and strategy thread can freeze, but orders already submitted to the broker are broker-side — they live at the exchange independently of NT's process state.
What survives a NT freeze
ComponentFreeze-safe?Reason
Open limit orders (entry)✓ YesResting at exchange
ATM stop orders✓ YesSubmitted to broker, not held in NT memory
ATM profit targets✓ YesSame — broker-side OCO group
ATM trailing stop⚠ PartialTrail logic runs in NT; stop order itself is broker-side. NT must reconnect to update trail.
Copier indicator logic✗ PausedC# indicator runs in NT process — new entries won't copy during freeze
Positions already open✓ YesBroker holds them regardless
Design choices that maximise resilience
DO Place entries via ChartTrader with ATM selected before clicking. ATM brackets submit atomically with the entry — the moment the order hits the broker, the bracket is already live.
DO Use fixed stops and targets in ATM rather than trailing-only. Fixed brackets are fully broker-side. Trailing requires NT to stay alive to move them.
NOTE If NT freezes after an entry fills but before the next new entry signal, all existing positions are protected by the broker-side bracket. Only future new copies are blocked.
FUTURE Phase 2 option: IPC watchdog process that re-submits orphaned entries on reconnect. Not needed at 2 trades/day.
Jane Street Rules — Agent DNA & Applied Decisions
Why this section exists: LLMs are trained on internet data — the internet is full of bad C# patterns (lock(), Dictionary<K,V> everywhere, throw in hot paths, null returns). These rules are distilled from Jane Street engineering practices and hardcoded into the DNA of every agent in the build pipeline — not just referenced as a file to read. Every agent role definition contains the full rule set verbatim so violations are structurally impossible to miss. This is the "make illegal states unrepresentable" principle applied to the agents, not just the code.

Full catalog: docs/standards/jane-street/RULES_CATALOG.md  |  Agent roles: .bob/custom_modes.yaml (ptt-architect, ptt-plan-reviewer, ptt-ticket-reviewer, ptt-engineer, ptt-verifier)
Complete JS rule set — active in every pipeline agent
RuleCategorySeverityPTT ImplementationIn Agent DNA
JS-001 No throw in hot paths Type Safety P0 SendCopy(), SendOrder(), gate chain all return bool. No throw in OnOrderUpdate path. Failures log to NT Output and return early. All 5 agents
JS-002 No null returns Type Safety P0 Guard-return pattern: if (order == null) return; at every boundary. No method returns null for a value the caller depends on. All 5 agents
JS-003 Sealed record hierarchies Type Safety P0 ATM mode is a discriminated union: abstract record FollowerAtmMode with sealed record Inherit / Market / Named. No magic strings for state. PositionState is a readonly struct. All 5 agents
JS-008 Readonly structs + Freeze() Immutability P1 readonly struct CopySignal, TrimSignal, PositionState. All SolidColorBrush semantic constants call .Freeze() — thread-safe for Dispatcher.InvokeAsync. All 5 agents
JS-009 ImmutableDictionary Immutability P1 ImmutableDictionary<string, FollowerAtmMode> on CopyRule (B7+). No mutable Dictionary<K,V> on any field touched by multiple threads. All 5 agents
JS-010 Private constructors Construction P1 private CopyEngine() singleton. All signal structs: private constructor + static Create() factory. Illegal states (null account + live order) are structurally unrepresentable. All 5 agents
JS-015 Parse at boundaries Parsing P2 Account name validated once in OnInitialize / Loaded. No re-validation in OnOrderUpdate hot path. All 5 agents
JS-021 No lock() Concurrency P0 Dedup: ConcurrentDictionary<string,long>. Toggle: volatile bool _isCopyEnabled. No Monitor, Mutex, SemaphoreSlim anywhere. SCAN-01 + SCAN-07 enforce at every build. All 5 agents
JS-023 Atomic primitives Concurrency P0 volatile bool _isCopyEnabled for on/off toggle. Interlocked for any numeric counter that crosses threads. UI callbacks: Dispatcher.InvokeAsync only. All 5 agents
JS-025 Lock-free collections Concurrency P1 ConcurrentDictionary for dedup TTL. ConcurrentBag<CopyRule> for rule list. No List<T> + manual lock anywhere. All 5 agents
How rules are enforced — 7-phase pipeline quality chain
PhaseAgentChecksGate
1 — Architect ptt-architect Designs to JS rules (DNA embedded). 8 sequential thoughts mandatory before writing. PLAN_FAILED if any JS violation in design
2 — Plan Review ptt-plan-reviewer Plan vs spec + requirements + full JS rule checklist. Every rule = explicit check. REVIEW_FAIL — re-architect max 2 cycles
3 — Ticket Gen ptt-architect Tickets cite spec req IDs, method JS constraints, xUnit names. Reads RULES_CATALOG.md. TICKETS_COMPLETE only if all items present
3.5 — Ticket Review NEW ptt-ticket-reviewer Tickets vs plan + spec + RULES_CATALOG. Traceability, JS pre-check, CYC pre-check, NT8 constraints, test coverage, file routing. TICKET_REVIEW_FAIL — re-generate tickets max 2 cycles
4a — Engineer ptt-engineer Writes code per ticket + plan. DNA embedded — writes JS-correct patterns by default. 7 scans to zero before BUILD_PASS. BUILD_FAIL — max 2 retries
4b — Verifier ptt-verifier Independent 7 scans + DNA rule hunt (lock, throw, null, magic string, mutable struct, unsealed brush, hex color, DateTime.Now). Checks implementation vs ticket AND plan AND spec. VERIFY_FAIL — max 3 retries
5 — Final Review ptt-plan-reviewer Cross-file coherence. All spec requirements end-to-end. All 7 scans zero on full directory. Section K deferred backlog. FINAL_FAIL — blocks PIPELINE_COMPLETE
NT8-specific rules — hardcoded into all agents (not in JS catalog)
RuleWhyEnforcement
No async/await in lifecycle methodsNT8 runs OnInitialize/OnDestroyed synchronously on the UI thread. async deadlocks.ptt-ticket-reviewer + ptt-verifier DNA
Account.All in Loaded onlyAccount list not available at construction time. Calling in ctor = null ref crash.ptt-ticket-reviewer + ptt-verifier DNA
TradeCopierWindow not sealedNT8 Window subclassing rule. sealed prevents NT8 from loading the class.SCAN-07 + ptt-verifier DNA
No FontFamily overrideInherit NT WPF theme. Overriding breaks the 100% NT-native appearance pillar.SCAN-03 — all phases
No #RRGGBB hex colorsNT dark/light theme switch breaks hardcoded hex. Use NTBrushes.* or frozen SolidColorBrush.SCAN-04 — all phases
"PTT-" prefix on all CreateOrder namesIsBracketLeg() Layer 2 guard — prevents self-cancellation race on CancelPendingEntries.SCAN-05 — all phases
DateTime.UtcNow not NowDeterministic timestamps. DateTime.Now is wall-clock dependent and non-reproducible.SCAN-06 — all phases
No letter-key KeyBindingNT8 WPF host rejects all Shift+letter KeyGesture combos at runtime.ptt-ticket-reviewer DNA
Math.Round(raw/tickSize)*tickSizeStop prices must be tick-aligned. Raw float price = invalid order rejection from broker.ptt-ticket-reviewer + ptt-verifier DNA
Technical Specification
File structure — 3 files, ~350 lines total
// Block 1: three files, one shared engine, two NT surfaces

// ── 1. CopyEngine.cs (424 lines) ── pure logic, zero UI refs
//    Singleton. Both surfaces share this exact instance.
internal sealed class CopyEngine
{
  private readonly struct CopyRule   // instrument + master + followers[] + Enabled (B3)
  private readonly struct CopySignal // action + type + qty + price + id (JS-008)
  private readonly struct TrimSignal // utcTime + instrument — NO qty (JS-003)

  OnOrderUpdate()          // gate chain → rule match → enabled check → dispatch
  SendCopy()               // CreateOrder + Submit → broker (hot potato)
  Trim()                   // ceil(own_qty/2) market order per account
  Flatten()                // full_qty market order per account (close all)
  CancelPendingEntries()   // cancel working entry orders only — not bracket legs
  BreakEven(instrument, bufferTicks)  // B4: move stop to entry price + N ticks (both surfaces)
  IsDedup()                // ConcurrentDictionary TTL (JS-025)
  SetEnabled(bool)         // called by either UI surface
  SetRuleEnabled(instrument, bool)  // B3: per-rule on/off toggle
  SetDailyCapFloor(double) // B3: configure P&L floor at runtime
  PassesDailyCapCheck()    // B3: real P&L gate via account.Get(RealizedPnL)
}

// ── 2. TradeCopierPanel.cs (251 lines) ── ChartTrader row
//    Primary surface. Lives inside ChartTrader next to Buy/Sell.
public sealed class TradeCopierPanel : NTWindow // ChartTrader extension
{
  OnInitialize()    // CopyEngine.Instance, bind to this chart's instrument
  OnDestroyed()     // unbind only — engine keeps running
  BuildUI()         // Row 1: [● COPY ON/OFF]
                    // Row 2: [Trim ½  S+T]  [Flatten  S+F]  [Cancel  S+C]  [BE  S+B] [2] tks
  OnToggle()        // CopyEngine.Instance.SetEnabled(bool)
  OnTrim()          // CopyEngine.Instance.Trim(instrument)
  OnFlatten()       // CopyEngine.Instance.Flatten(instrument)
  OnCancel()        // CopyEngine.Instance.CancelPendingEntries(instrument)
  OnBreakEven()     // B4: CopyEngine.Instance.BreakEven(instrument, bufferTicks)
}

// ── 3. TradeCopierWindow.cs (462 lines) ── Add-On window
//    Secondary surface. Rule management + status log.
public class TradeCopierWindow : NTWindow
{
  OnInitialize()       // CopyEngine.Instance (same singleton) + Subscribe()
  BuildUI()            // global on/off + rule display + status log
  BuildRuleRow()       // static row: instr label + accounts + [½][=][x][ON] + Apply + [BE][2]tks
  BuildDynamicRuleRow() // B3: dynamic row with editable TextBox instrument name
  OnRuleBreakEven()    // B4: reads instrName + buffer from Tag, calls engine.BreakEven
  OnStatusUpdate()     // engine fires event → Dispatcher.InvokeAsync → log line
}

// ── 4. CopyEngineTests.cs (264 lines) ── xUnit test suite (B3-B5)
public class CopyEngineTests  // 17 [Fact] methods — xUnit only, namespace PropTraderTools
{
  // Gate-1, DailyCapFloor, SetRuleEnabled, AddRule, StatusUpdate,
  // IsDedup (via reflection), Flatten/CancelPendingEntries null-instrument smoke tests
}
▶ Named Requirement: Cross-Surface State Sync — Every UI stays in real-time sync with every other UI
DESIGN CONTRACT — APPLIES TO ALL SURFACES, ALL BLOCKS
Rule: No UI surface may hold state. State lives only in CopyEngine. Every UI surface is a read-only projection of engine state — it reflects what the engine says, not what the user last clicked on that surface.

Consequence: Click Copy ON in the ChartTrader panel → engine state changes → engine fires CopyEnabledChanged event → all subscribed surfaces (panel, window, any future 3rd surface) update simultaneously. The panel that fired the click does not set its own button colour — the engine's event does. This eliminates the "I toggled it here but the other window didn't update" class of bug entirely.
✗ NAÏVE PATTERN — causes drift
// Panel: toggle fires, panel updates itself
void OnCopyToggle()
{
    _engine.SetEnabled(!_engine.IsEnabled);
    // Panel updates its own button here
    _btnCopy.Background = _engine.IsEnabled
        ? Brushes.Green : Brushes.DarkGray;
    // Window never notified — it stays stale.
}
ChartTrader panel shows ON.
Add-On window still shows OFF.
Add a 3rd surface → 3 places to update manually.
State can diverge on any crash/reload.
✓ EVENT-DRIVEN PATTERN — always in sync
// Panel: toggle fires, calls engine ONLY
void OnCopyToggle()
    => _engine.SetEnabled(!_engine.IsEnabled);
    // Panel does NOT update itself here.

// CopyEngine.SetEnabled() fires event:
internal void SetEnabled(bool value)
{
    _isCopyEnabled = value;
    CopyEnabledChanged?.Invoke(value);  // ← notify all
}

// Both Panel AND Window subscribe at init:
_engine.CopyEnabledChanged += OnCopyEnabledChanged;

// Handler (same pattern on both surfaces):
void OnCopyEnabledChanged(bool enabled)
    => Dispatcher.InvokeAsync(()
        => _btnCopy.Background = enabled
            ? Brushes.Green : Brushes.DarkGray);
All surfaces update simultaneously.
Adding a 3rd surface = subscribe + implement handler.
No surface can ever be stale — it reads from the event, not from memory.
Complete CopyEngine event inventory — all surfaces subscribe to all relevant events
Event Signature Fired when Panel reacts Window reacts
CopyEnabledChanged Action<bool> SetEnabled() is called from any surface Copy toggle button → green/grey + label "Copy ON" / "Copy OFF" Global copy toggle in window → same green/grey update
PositionStateChanged Action<Instrument,
bool hasPos,
bool hasEntries>		 NT8 ExecutionUpdate or PositionUpdate on leader account Flatten/Cancel/Trim button enabled state (grey when flat) Per-rule row status colour + position label
StatusUpdated Action<string> Any engine action (copy sent, trim, flatten, error) — Status log TextBlock in window appends line
RuleEnabledChanged Action<Instrument, bool> SetRuleEnabled() called from any surface — Per-rule [ON/OFF] toggle button colour in window
CopyModeChanged Action<CopyMode> SetCopyMode() called from any surface [Signal] / [Mirror] radio buttons reflect new mode Mode display in window rule row updates
Threading contract — engine fires on NT8 thread, surfaces marshal to UI thread
Engine side Events fire on whatever thread SetEnabled / SetRuleEnabled / SetCopyMode was called from — could be UI thread (user click) or NT8 worker thread (programmatic). The engine does NOT marshal — it is UI-ignorant. Surface side Every event handler on every surface wraps its WPF update in Dispatcher.InvokeAsync(). This is always safe — calling InvokeAsync from the UI thread is a no-op, from a worker thread it marshals correctly. NT8 rule No async/await in NT8 lifecycle methods (OnInitialize/OnDestroyed deadlock — NT8-P0). Use Dispatcher.InvokeAsync only. The CopyEnabledChanged handler is a plain void method that wraps in InvokeAsync — CYC=1.
Adding a 3rd surface (e.g. floating dashboard, web socket feed, future NT8 Market Replay overlay) — 3 steps only
Step 1 In OnInitialize(): subscribe to all relevant events on CopyEngine.Instance. Step 2 Implement each handler to update that surface's UI via Dispatcher.InvokeAsync. Step 3 In OnDestroyed(): unsubscribe all events — prevents memory leaks and ghost handlers on NT8 reload cycles.
The engine is not modified at all. No new code paths. No new conditionals. The surface just listens.
NT8 lifecycle critical: NT8 reloads the NinjaScript domain on every F5 recompile. The old surface instance's event subscriptions must be removed in OnDestroyed() using -=. If subscriptions are not removed, the old (dead) surface handler fires on the new engine instance — causing ghost UI updates and null-ref crashes on NT8 reload. This is the same pattern already used for AccountItemUpdate unsubscription in FollowerItem.
Named Requirement: Cross-Surface Sync ✓ Engine fires — surfaces listen. Never the reverse. 5 events: CopyEnabledChanged, PositionStateChanged, StatusUpdated, RuleEnabledChanged, CopyModeChanged All handlers: Dispatcher.InvokeAsync — never async/await OnDestroyed() MUST unsubscribe — NT8 reload safety
Event handler decision table
EventCondition to copyOrder sent to followers
OrderState.Submitted
+ IsMarket == true		 Copy enabled, dedup pass, P&L pass Market order — same qty
OrderState.Submitted
+ IsLimit == true		 Copy enabled, dedup pass, P&L pass Limit order — same limit price, same qty
OrderState.Submitted
+ IsStopMarket == true		 Ignored — ATM handles stops natively — not copied —
OrderState.Cancelled Ignored — ATM OCO handles cancellation — not copied —
Any order from a follower account Always ignored (source check: account == master) — never copies a copy —
NT-Native UI Specification — "Built internally" standard
Pillar: 100% NT-native appearance. Every control uses NT's own WPF resource dictionary. No custom colors, no custom fonts, no custom borders. A customer should look at this panel and think it shipped with NinjaTrader.

Break-Even Button (B3/B4 feature)
AttributeDetail
UI placement "BE S+B" button in the TradeCopierWindow rule row and in the TradeCopierPanel action row. Sits immediately after the Cancel button. Accompanied by an inline buffer TextBox (default "2") and a tks label.
Handler Clicking the button invokes OnBreakEvenClicked (Panel) or OnRuleBreakEven (Window), which reads the inline buffer TextBox and calls CopyEngine.Instance.BreakEven(instrument, bufferTicks).
Behavior Moves the active stop loss on all open positions (master + all followers for the active instrument) to the entry price plus bufferTicks ticks. Uses order.Change() on every open stop leg detected by IsStopLeg(). Price is rounded to the instrument tick size via Math.Round.
Buffer field Integer tick count. Default 2. Live-editable on the panel during trading without any properties dialog (Control-from-outside pillar). Setting it to 0 moves the stop exactly to entry price.
Scope Acts on master + all followers for the instrument of the active rule (Panel: current chart instrument; Window: rule under mouse cursor tracked by _activeRuleInstrument). Two instruments are never moved simultaneously.
Shift+B Keyboard Shortcut (B4/B5 feature)
AttributeDetail
Keyboard binding Shift+B — registered as a WPF KeyBinding inside TradeCopierWindow.OnInitialize().
Registration using System.Windows.Input is added to the Window. The binding uses a RelayCommand nested class wired to the OnWindowBreakEven handler method.
Handler OnWindowBreakEven in TradeCopierWindow. Reads _activeRuleInstrument (set by MouseEnter on each rule row) and the associated buffer TextBox value, then calls CopyEngine.Instance.BreakEven(instrument, bufferTicks).
Scope Active only when the TradeCopierWindow has WPF keyboard focus. Behavior is identical to clicking the [BE] button on the focused rule row.
Panel equivalent TradeCopierPanel (ChartTrader row) has its own Shift+B KeyBinding added in B4 via the standard ChartTrader input binding mechanism, wired to OnBreakEven.
Follower Account Selection — evolution B1 → B7
BlockControlSurfaceNotes
B1–B4 Single-select ComboBox Panel + Window One follower account only.
B5–B6 ListBox SelectionMode.Extended + ScrollViewer MaxHeight=80 Panel + Window Multi-select. Ctrl+click to pick multiple. ScrollViewer caps height.
B7 Checkmark ComboBox — header = "N selected" — row = [name][P&L][✓] Panel only Live Map design pillar. Compact — collapses when not in use. Header shows live selection count at all times: "0 selected", "2 selected", etc — never a static prompt. Each row: Grid (6 fixed columns) — left to right: account name (star) | daily P&L (62px) | mult (30px) | ATM (80px) | named (80px) | checkmark (20px). All columns align vertically across rows regardless of account name length (OnRowGridLoaded pattern — B10-UI-01). P&L is live — pushed by NT8 Account.AccountItemUpdate event (AccountItem.RealizedProfitLoss), no custom calculation. Color-coded: green (+$x.xx), red (-$x.xx), dim ($0.00). FollowerItem implements INotifyPropertyChanged — DailyPnlText + DailyPnlColor bindings update without full list refresh. Dispatcher.InvokeAsync in OnAccountItemUpdate — JS-023 compliant. Subscribed in OnLoaded, unsubscribed in Detach() — no leak. GetSelectedFollowers() iterates _followerItems — no SelectedItems dependency. Window rule rows retain ListBox (B5 pattern) — sufficient vertical space in Window. CopyEngine.AddRule(Account[]) unchanged — UI-only change.
Stop Buffer Field (B5 feature)
AttributeDetail
UI control Integer TextBox labeled "Buffer" (or shown inline as [2] tks) adjacent to the [BE] button on both Panel and Window.
Purpose Adds a configurable tick offset to the copied stop loss distance. Stored as the StopBuffer field of CopyRule.
Effect When StopBuffer > 0, the follower stop loss is placed StopBuffer ticks further from entry than the source stop loss. When StopBuffer == 0 the stop is copied exactly (no offset).
Default 0 (no buffer -- stop copied at exact source distance). Default display value in the TextBox is "2" as a suggested starting point but the engine-level default is 0 until the user applies a rule.
Live-editable Buffer is readable and writable during an active trading session without restarting or using any properties dialog (Control-from-outside pillar).
B6 Rule Persistence (XML)
New in B6. Copy rules now survive NinjaTrader restarts. Rules are serialized to an XML file on shutdown and deserialized on startup using the NT lifecycle hooks. No NuGet packages required -- uses System.Xml.Serialization.XmlSerializer built into .NET Framework 4.8 (the NT8 runtime).
AttributeDetail
Format XML via System.Xml.Serialization.XmlSerializer (NOT JSON). File name: copy_rules.xml.
File location {NinjaTrader.Core.Globals.UserDataDir}\PropTraderTools\copy_rules.xml. Path is computed via Path.Combine() to handle trailing separator differences across NT installations. The PropTraderTools subdirectory is created by Directory.CreateDirectory() on first save.
Save trigger TradeCopierWindow.OnDestroyed() -- the NT lifecycle hook called on NT shutdown or when the Add-On window is closed. Calls CopyEngine.Instance.SaveRules() as its very first statement (before any UI cleanup).
Load trigger TradeCopierWindow.OnInitialize() -- called once at NT startup. Calls CopyEngine.Instance.LoadRules() after all existing init logic, then triggers the rule-list UI refresh so loaded rules are visible immediately. A _persistenceLoaded volatile bool guard prevents duplicate-add if OnInitialize is ever called more than once.
Fields persisted SourceAccountName, FollowerAccountNames[], LotRatio, TickOffset, StopBuffer, IsEnabled. Serialized via private nested DTO classes (CopyRuleDto, CopyRulesContainer) inside CopyEngine.
No-file guard LoadRules() is a no-op when copy_rules.xml does not exist (first run after install). No exception is thrown.
Thread safety No lock() required -- both SaveRules() and LoadRules() are called exclusively on the NT main thread at lifecycle boundaries (shutdown/startup). Rules are added to ConcurrentBag<CopyRule> via iterative .Add() -- the field is never reassigned.
OnOrderUpdate — core dispatch logic
private void OnOrderUpdate(object sender, OrderEventArgs e)
{
  // Gate 1: copy enabled?
  if (!_isCopyEnabled) return;

  // Gate 2: find matching rule (instrument + master account)
  CopyRule? matchedRule = null;
  foreach (var rule in _rules)
  {
    if (e.Order.Instrument.FullName == rule.Instrument && e.Order.Account == rule.MasterAccount)
    { matchedRule = rule; break; }
  }
  if (matchedRule == null) return;

  // Gate 3: only on Submitted, only market or limit
  if (e.OrderState != OrderState.Submitted) return;
  if (!e.Order.IsMarket && !e.Order.IsLimit) return;

  // Gate 4: dedup (JS-025 ConcurrentDictionary, JS-023 Interlocked)
  if (IsDedup(e.Order.OrderId)) return;

  // Build immutable signal (JS-008)
  var signal = new CopySignal(
    e.Order.OrderAction, e.Order.OrderType,
    e.Order.Quantity, e.Order.LimitPrice, e.Order.OrderId
  );

  // Dispatch to each follower
  foreach (var acc in _followers)
  {
    if (acc == null) continue;
    if (!PassesDailyCapCheck(acc)) continue;
    SendCopy(acc, signal);   // returns bool, logs on false (JS-001)
  }
}
Flatten() — close full position, all accounts
internal void Flatten(Instrument instrument)
{
  // Same mechanics as Trim — no qty in signal, each account reads its own
  foreach (var acc in AllAccounts(instrument))  // master + followers
  {
    var pos = acc.Positions.FindByInstrument(instrument);
    if (pos == null || pos.Quantity == 0) { Log("flat skip"); continue; }

    var action = pos.MarketPosition == MarketPosition.Long
      ? OrderAction.Sell : OrderAction.BuyToCover;

    acc.CreateOrder(instrument, action, OrderType.Market,
      OrderEntry.Manual, TimeInForce.Day, pos.Quantity, 0, 0, null,
      "PTT-Flatten", DateTime.MaxValue, null)
      .Submit();   // hot potato — broker handles the rest
  }
}
CancelPendingEntries() — pull unfilled entries, never bracket legs
internal void CancelPendingEntries(Instrument instrument)
{
  foreach (var acc in AllAccounts(instrument))  // master + followers
  {
    foreach (var order in acc.Orders)
    {
      // Only working orders on this instrument
      if (order.Instrument != instrument)               continue;
      if (order.OrderState != OrderState.Working &&
          order.OrderState != OrderState.PartialFilled)  continue;

      // Skip bracket legs — stops and targets carry ATM-stamped names
      // or a non-null FromEntrySignal reference (set by ATM at attach time)
      if (IsBracketLeg(order))                          continue;

      order.Cancel();   // NT API — one call, broker handles confirmation
      Log($"{acc.Name}: entry pulled {order.OrderId}");
    }
  }
}

private bool IsBracketLeg(Order order)
{
  // ATM bracket legs are stamped: "Stop1", "Target1", "Target2", etc.
  // or carry a non-null FromEntrySignal (set by ATM at attach time)
  return order.FromEntrySignal != null
      || (order.Name != null &&
          (order.Name.StartsWith("Stop")   ||
           order.Name.StartsWith("Target") ||
           order.Name.StartsWith("PTT-")));  // our own orders — never cancel
}
AllAccounts() — the scope helper used by Trim, Flatten, and Cancel
// Single source of truth for "which accounts does this instrument rule cover".
// Every action method (Trim, Flatten, CancelPendingEntries) calls this.
// Changing the scope of any action = change it here once.
private IEnumerable<Account> AllAccounts(Instrument instrument)
{
  var rule = FindRule(instrument);   // O(n) over the rule list — tiny
  if (rule == null) yield break;

  yield return rule.MasterAccount;           // leader always included
  foreach (var acc in rule.FollowerAccounts)  // all checked followers
    if (acc != null) yield return acc;

  // Instrument fence: only accounts in THIS rule are returned.
  // MES cancel never sees MNQ accounts — even if the master account
  // is shared between rules. The rule lookup is keyed on instrument.
}
Cancel Safety Guarantees — three independent layers
CancelPendingEntries() will never cancel the stop or profit target of an open position. Three independent guards enforce this. All three must pass for an order to be cancelled.
LayerGuardWhat it protectsReliability
1 — Primary order.FromEntrySignal != null Every ATM bracket leg — stops AND targets, all ATM templates. NT's ATM engine sets FromEntrySignal on every bracket order at attach time. It is not user-configurable and cannot be blank on an ATM bracket. ✓ NT-enforced. Structural.
2 — Our prefix name.StartsWith("PTT-") Our own submitted orders — PTT-Flatten, PTT-Trim, etc. Prevents a race condition where Cancel fires while a Flatten order we just submitted is still Working. ✓ We control all our order names.
3 — Belt-and-suspenders name.StartsWith("Stop")
name.StartsWith("Target")		 Manually-placed bracket orders (not via ATM) that use NT's default naming. Layer 1 does not protect these because manual brackets have FromEntrySignal == null. ⚠ Name-dependent. See caveat below.
Known caveat — manual brackets with custom names: If a user places a manual stop (without ATM) and renames it to something that doesn't start with "Stop" or "Target", Layer 3 will not catch it and Layer 1 won't fire (no FromEntrySignal). That order would be cancelled.

Mitigation: The tooltip on the Cancel button reads: "Cancels pending unfilled entries only. ATM brackets are always protected (Layer 1). Manual stops/targets are protected if using NT default names." Users who rely on manual custom-named brackets should not use this button — or should use ATM, which is already recommended.
Per-instrument scope — Cancel acts on leader + all followers
AccountIncluded in Cancel?Why
Master (leader) account ✓ Always If you cancel, you want your own pending entry pulled too. Leaving master in-queue while followers are cleared breaks the rule asymmetrically.
Follower accounts (checked) ✓ Always All accounts in rule.FollowerAccounts — the same set used for copy and trim.
Accounts in a different instrument's rule ✗ Never AllAccounts(instrument) returns only accounts belonging to the rule keyed on that instrument. MES Cancel never sees MNQ accounts — even if the master account is shared between rules.
Accounts not in any rule ✗ Never Only accounts explicitly listed in a rule's master + follower set are iterated. Other connected accounts are invisible to the engine.

Click Trader — Chart Click → Exact Price Limit Order
✓ DW-B17-01 CLOSED (B17) — Click trader fully operational at runtime. Root cause: NT8 chart canvas sets e.Handled = true on MouseDown, suppressing the event before it reaches the Add-On overlay. Secondary cause: FindVisualChild<ChartPanel> DFS found the ChartTrader sidebar panel (ActualWidth=139.33, MaxValue=0) instead of the price canvas. B17 fix: All RegisterClickTrader / UnregisterClickTrader calls changed to PreviewMouseDown (Director-authorized T1-Amendment). FindPriceCanvasPanel() added — selects widest ChartPanel with MaxValue > 0 (T2). 7 new [Fact] tests. Director confirmed live: click trader places limit orders at correct chart price.
Click Trader — design decisions
DecisionValueRationale
Order price Exact chart click price — tick-aligned Math.Round(rawPrice / tickSize) * tickSize. No spread offset. No Bid+1/Ask-1 adjustment. You click the price you want; that is the limit price.
Order type Limit Click trader is a precision entry tool. Market orders defeat the purpose of clicking a specific price level.
Bid+1/Ask-1 auto-offset Not implemented (DW-B9-03 shelved) The one-tick spread offset is a separate future feature (DW-B9-03). Shelved until DW-B8-04 is closed. B15 scope = exact price only.
Price lookup method ⚠ B17 OPEN — DW-B17-01 B16 T1 walked ChartPanel depth=2 finding ContentPresenter (sidebar panel, ActualWidth=139.33). Branch B uses MaxValue/MinValue from this wrong instance — both return 0 at runtime. Price canvas ChartPanel (~1050px wide) never found. B17 T1 enumerates ALL ChartPanels; T2 picks widest with MaxValue > 0 (Option A) or uses ChartControl.Charts collection if available (Option B, preferred).
Quantity ATR-sized via GetSuggestedQty() Reuses the same sizing path as copy dispatch — consistent sizing across all entry methods.
Direction Controlled by [Buy]/[Sell] ToggleButton pair volatile bool _clickBuy — set by the ToggleButton, read by OnChartMouseDown (JS-023 compliant).
Signal name "PTT-Click" Starts with "PTT-" prefix — required so IsBracketLeg / CancelPendingEntries layer 2 guard never cancels a click-placed entry. NT8 constraint: all our CreateOrder names must start "PTT-".
Current price state ⚠ Interim: GetRefPrice() fallback (B17 T1) B16 compile passed but runtime returns 0 — guard (5) fires, no order placed. B17 T1 adds GetRefPrice() fallback to OnChartMouseDown so click trader fires at last-trade price while T1 investigates the correct ChartPanel. B17 T2 replaces with FindPriceCanvasPanel() using confirmed NT8 path.

The TradeCopierPanel itself is placed in a new row via grid.RowDefinitions.Add + Grid.SetRow — that part is correct. Fix: move ATR label INTO the TradeCopierPanel UI (as a row in _contentPanel) so it renders BELOW the native ChartTrader controls, not injected into their Grid cell. BuildAtrOverlayRow and the _atrOverlayLabel field in TradeCopierAddOn are removed. UpdateAtrOverlay delegates to TradeCopierPanel.SetAtrText(string) instead. No new [Fact] required (runtime-only UI positioning; tested manually F5). TradeCopierAddOn.cs, TradeCopierPanel.cs ✓ CLOSED (B20 Lane C T5)
DW-B20-CHARTTRADER-01  ·  P1  ·  ChartTrader native buttons blocked by ATR overlay row injection
Root Cause — grounded in source (TradeCopierAddOn.cs)
Two injection sites in DoInject / StartAtrEngine:
(1) TradeCopierPanel — appended correctly as a new Grid row via grid.RowDefinitions.Add(new RowDefinition{Height=Auto}) + Grid.SetRow(panel, grid.RowDefinitions.Count-1). This pushes the panel BELOW the NT8 native rows. ✓ Correct.

(2) ATR overlay Border — appended via chartTraderRoot.Children.Add(border) where chartTraderRoot = chartTrader.Content as Panel. No Grid.SetRow call. When Content is a Grid (which it is — confirmed in DoInject stale-purge block), a child added without Grid.SetRow defaults to row 0 — the same row as the NT8 Buy/Sell/Close order-entry buttons. The ATR Border renders on top of (or forces layout reflow of) the native button row. ✗ Broken.
Why Native Buttons May Stop Working
Three likely mechanisms (any one is sufficient):
  • Hit-test blocking — WPF hit-testing is Z-order-first. A non-transparent Border overlapping the button area intercepts mouse clicks before they reach the NT8 buttons.
  • Row 0 height inflation — RowDefinition Height=Auto in a Grid measures all children in the row. If ATR Border forces row 0 taller, the NT8 buttons may be partially clipped by the ChartTrader viewport.
  • Double-injection on F5 — stale-purge removes TradeCopierPanel children but NOT the ATR Border. Each F5 appends another ATR Border to row 0, progressively worsening the overlap.
Fix Design (T5 scope)
Remove BuildAtrOverlayRow from TradeCopierAddOn. Add a SetAtrText(string display) method to TradeCopierPanel that updates an existing TextBlock already inside _contentPanel (the collapsible container — already has a row at the bottom for Risk/ATR row from B12-T3). TradeCopierAddOn.UpdateAtrOverlay → calls panel.SetAtrText(display) via Dispatcher.InvokeAsync (same pattern as today). The ATR text becomes part of the Panel's own layout — rendered BELOW the NT8 native rows, never in row 0. Stale-purge in DoInject already handles Panel re-injection; ATR label travels with the Panel — no separate purge needed.
Scope Boundary for T5
In scope: Move ATR display into TradeCopierPanel. Remove BuildAtrOverlayRow + _atrOverlayLabel from AddOn. Wire SetAtrText in Panel. Update UpdateAtrOverlay in AddOn to delegate to panel.SetAtrText.
Out of scope: Do NOT change ATR computation, polling timer, engine wiring, or StartAtrEngine logic. Do NOT touch TradeCopierWindow. Do NOT reposition TradeCopierPanel relative to ChartTrader — only the ATR label needs to move.

B22 DEFECT LOG
ID Description Priority Status
DW-B22-NULLREF-01 PTT-Copy error: Object reference not set to an instance of an object — NullReferenceException inside acc.CreateOrder() on non-chart-active follower accounts.
Confirmed from TradeCopierWindow log (B22 screenshot): Multiple PTT-Copy error: Object reference not set to an instance of an object at 00:38:05, 00:37:08, 00:36:35, 00:35:12. These are thrown by NT8 inside follower.CreateOrder() (CopyEngine.cs line 741) and caught at line 757 → logged via StatusUpdate.
Root cause — NT8 internal NullRef on non-active-chart accounts: NT8's Account.CreateOrder() internally dereferences a chart-context object (e.g. BarsArray, BarsPeriod, or an internal dispatcher reference) that is only non-null for the account owning the active chart. For all other accounts the internal object is null → NullReferenceException thrown inside NT8 internals → caught by our try/catch → order never submitted.
This is why PA-APEX-04 (active chart account) succeeded and PA-APEX-03/06 threw NullRef. Same mechanism applies to Sim102 vs Sim101.
The orders shown as Initialized on PA-APEX-03/06 are from the stale persisted rule (loaded at startup via LoadRules()) — those were submitted in a previous session when those accounts may have been the active chart at that time. The B22 copy attempts to those accounts threw NullRef and never submitted.
Fix direction — must solve non-active-chart submission: NT8 forum / known pattern: use NinjaTrader.NinjaScript.StrategyBase as submission context (requires a companion strategy), OR investigate whether NinjaTrader.Cbi.Connection.PlaybackConnection / NinjaTrader.Cbi.Account.CreateOrder behaves differently when called from a Dispatcher.InvokeAsync UI-thread context vs. OrderUpdate callback thread. Alternative: call Dispatcher.InvokeAsync for every follower CreateOrder call — NT8 may require order submission on the UI thread for non-active-chart accounts.
Workaround (test only): Use only the active chart account as follower. All others will throw NullRef. 		P0 ✗ OPEN — blocks all multi-account copying. Primary target for B23. Director approval needed.
DW-B22-ADDRULE-ACCUMULATE-01 AddRule() accumulates into ConcurrentBag without replacing existing (instrument, leader) rule — stale persisted followers fire on every session.
Confirmed from code (CopyEngine.cs lines 307–322): Both AddRule overloads call _rules.Add() unconditionally — no replace-if-exists logic. On startup LoadRules() (line 1561) calls _rules.Add(DtoToRule(dto)) for every saved rule. When Director clicks Apply Rule, OnApplyRule calls engine.AddRule() → adds a second rule for the same instrument+leader. Gate 2 in OnOrderUpdate matches on first hit and breaks (matchedRule = rule; break; line 389) — so only one rule fires per order update. The persisted rule fires first (it was added first by LoadRules).
Consequence: After PA session, XML persists PA-APEX-03/04/06 as followers. Next session: LoadRules restores PA rule. Apply Rule with Sim102 adds second rule — but the PA rule fires first. Copies go to PA accounts (which throw NullRef) instead of Sim102.
Fix (minimal): In AddRule() 5-arg overload: rebuild ConcurrentBag removing any existing rule where r.Instrument == instrument && r.MasterAccount?.Name == master?.Name before adding the new rule. Same ConcurrentBag-rebuild pattern already used by SetFollowerMultiplier() (lines 330–344). 		P1 ✗ OPEN — fix ticket B23-T1. Requires Director approval.
DW-B22-BE-TRIGGER-01 BE Armed mode fires on UnrealizedPnL ≥ 0 — but PA prop accounts start with a commission/fee deduction making UPnL negative even when price is past entry. BE never triggers in practice.
How BE works (3-state FSM):
Idle → click BE button → Armed: subscribes to masterAcc.AccountItemUpdate. Watches UnrealizedProfitLoss. Fires BreakEven() as soon as e.Value >= 0 (CopyEngine.cs line 1356).
When BE fires → state transitions to BE Live: stop is moved to entry ± bufferTicks × tickSize via acc.Change(). Then ArmTrailBe() is called — the trail watcher continuously advances the stop as UnrealizedPnL makes new highs.
Click BE again from Live → disarms both watchers → back to Idle.
The defect: The Armed trigger condition is if (e.Value < 0) return; — fires when UPnL ≥ 0. On PA accounts, the moment you enter a trade NT8 deducts commission. UPnL immediately goes negative (e.g. -$2.50 for 1 MES). Even when price moves past your entry, UPnL may still be slightly negative due to bid/ask spread + commission. The Armed watcher never sees UPnL ≥ 0 until you are sufficiently in profit — potentially long after you have already passed BE price level. Director observed: armed, moved past BE price, nothing happened.
Fix direction: Change Armed trigger from e.Value < 0 (dollar PnL) to a price-based trigger: compare instrument.MarketData.Last.Price against pos.AveragePrice ± bufferTicks × tickSize. Fire when price reaches entry + buffer — not when dollar PnL crosses zero. This is immune to commission deductions and more intuitive.
Note: MoveStopToBreakEven() itself is correct — it moves stop to pos.AveragePrice + direction × bufferTicks × tickSize. Only the Armed trigger condition is wrong. 		P1 ⚠ OPEN — fix ticket B23-T1. Director approval needed.

◯ PENDING EXECUTION Baseline [Fact] count: 122 Target [Fact] count: 126 SRC CODE BAN — all edits go through ptt-orchestrator → lane workers Parallel: Lanes A / B / C may run simultaneously (no shared edit sites)
WORKFLOW-ADVISORY-01  ·  ctx MCP parallel scan loop & PTT parallel lane limits  ·  CONFIRMED
Root Cause — Confirmed
lean-ctx uses a single shared SQLite store under %LOCALAPPDATA%/lean-ctx/. Every ctx_shell, ctx_session, and ctx_knowledge remember call writes to that store. SQLite WAL allows many concurrent readers but only one writer at a time. When two parallel lane sessions both call any ctx write tool simultaneously, one gets SQLITE_BUSY. The MCP server wraps this as an error response — the agent sees failure, retries immediately, hits the same lock → 100+ back-to-back retry loop. This applies to ctx_shell (tee log write) as well as ctx_session and ctx_knowledge.
Why Scans Are the Worst Offender
The 7-scan chain calls ctx_shell 7 times in sequence per lane. If another lane is doing the same thing simultaneously, their ctx_shell writes race for the SQLite lock every single call. With two lanes running 7 scans each = 14 concurrent write attempts → near-100% collision rate. The solution is not to batch the scans — it is to never run scans across two lanes at the same clock instant (sequential start stagger) AND to use execute_command as the fallback when ctx_shell errors.
Three Rules — All Mandatory in Every Lane Prompt
# Rule Detail
R1 Scans are always sequential within a lane Call one ctx_shell scan, wait for result, then call the next. Never batch 7 scans in one tool call. This is already in the mode instructions — agents must obey it unconditionally.
R2 ctx_shell MCP/transport error → execute_command fallback (once) Distinguish two error types:
  • MCP/transport error — ctx_shell returns no output at all, or an MCP error code (SQLITE_BUSY, timeout, tool crash). This is the loop trigger. Fix: call execute_command with the exact same command. Do NOT retry ctx_shell. Continue remaining scans normally.
  • Command output error — ctx_shell ran fine and the command itself returned an error (e.g. build failed, grep found a violation, dotnet test failed). This is a legitimate failure. Read the output, understand the error, fix the code. Do NOT fall back to execute_command — the problem is in the code, not the tool.
R3 ctx writes only at phase boundaries, unique key per lane ctx_session task / ctx_knowledge remember only at the START and END of a lane — never mid-scan. Each lane uses a unique key: Lane A → --key B20-laneA-t1-build; Lane B → --key B20-laneB-t4-complete. Never share a key across concurrent lanes.
How Many PTT Orchestrator Lanes in Parallel?
The real constraint is file contention in src/PropTraderTools/, not session count. Two lanes that write to the same .cs file = merge conflict or corruption. Two lanes that write to different files = fully safe to run in parallel.
File ownership rule: Before starting any lane, assign it a disjoint file set. No file may appear in more than one active lane's write-set at the same time. The orchestrator is responsible for this assignment — it must verify no overlap before spawning.
B20 example — 3-lane map (2 parallel + 1 sequential tail)
Lane Tickets Write-set (.cs files) Can start in parallel?
Lane A T1 then T2 (sequential within lane) CopyEngine.cs, CopyEngineTests.cs ✓ Yes — start immediately
Lane B T4 (doc only) NT8_COMPILER_RULES.md, NT8_ADDON_KNOWLEDGE.md only ✓ Yes — start immediately (no .cs contention)
Lane C T3 then T5 (sequential within lane) TradeCopierPanel.cs, TradeCopierWindow.cs, TradeCopierAddOn.cs ⚠ Wait for Lane A T2 VERIFY_PASS
(T2 also wires Panel + Window → must finish first)
General Formula — N safe parallel lanes
  1. List all files each ticket touches (writes, not reads).
  2. Cluster tickets into groups with non-overlapping write-sets — each group = one lane.
  3. If a ticket in Group A writes to a file that Group B also writes to → merge them into one lane (sequential within).
  4. Stagger lane starts by 15 seconds — prevents ctx_session task writes from colliding at the exact same moment.
  5. Maximum practical parallel lanes for PTT: 3. Beyond 3 the SQLite write pressure increases and the human-in-the-loop overhead of tracking concurrent sessions exceeds the time saved.
Scan fallback pattern — paste into any lane prompt:
SCAN RULE: Run each of the 7 scans with ctx_shell, one at a time, sequential. If ctx_shell returns an error (any error), immediately re-run that same command with execute_command instead. Do NOT retry ctx_shell for the same scan. execute_command is the fallback — it always produces output, it never loops. After the fallback succeeds, continue remaining scans with ctx_shell normally.

Modular Gate Architecture — plug features in/out
Decision: Build the copier now. Design the dispatch path as a linear gate chain — each feature is one isolated check that returns early. Adding a license gate, a tier gate, or a remote kill-switch later is a one-line insertion. No architecture needed today.
⚠ Named Requirement: No Spaghetti Code — Modules Ship, Disable, and Sell Independently
P0 ARCHITECTURAL CONSTRAINT — APPLIES TO ALL BLOCKS
Every module in the PTT suite — Copier, Action Buttons, ATR Engine, Risk Manager — must be designed and implemented so that:
  • Disabling one module at runtime has zero effect on any other module's behaviour.
  • Removing one module from a compiled build produces zero compiler errors in the remaining modules.
  • Each module can be sold separately at its own price point with its own license gate — without forking the codebase.
  • No module may new or static-call into another module's internals. All cross-module communication goes through an interface or event contract — never a hard type reference.
Module independence matrix
Module Standalone product? Price point Can be disabled without affecting… Cross-module contract
Trade Copier ✓ Yes $149 lifetime Action Buttons, ATR Engine, Risk Manager ICopySignalSource — fires on order submitted; ATR engine subscribes optionally
Action Buttons ✓ Yes $49 lifetime Copier, ATR Engine, Risk Manager IChartTraderBridge — issues Trim/Flatten/MoveStop commands; copier mirrors independently

Removing any one of these = cascade of compiler errors.

✓ MODULAR — our pattern
// CopyEngine calls provider interface (null-safe):

// ActionButton fires event; copier subscribes optionally:
_bridge.OnFlatten += HandleFlattenSignal;  // or not
// RiskManager implements IPnlGate — no NT8 UI imports:
public bool CanTrade(Account acc) => ...;
Remove ATR Engine: _atrProvider = null → manual mode.
Remove Risk Manager: _pnlGate = AlwaysPass → no change to copier.
Each ships, sells, and disables cleanly.

Dashed arrows = optional interface wire-up at startup. Present when module licensed; swapped to null-provider when not. The gate chain (linear if (!x) return; pattern) uses interface calls at each gate — a null implementation is a pass-through gate. One compiled binary. Zero code forks.
NT8 compiler note: Interface contracts are plain C# interfaces — fully supported in NT8 (.NET 4.8 Roslyn). No abstract record, no init properties, no ImmutableDictionary — the interface pattern uses only pre-C#9 syntax (NT8-001/NT8-002 safe). Each module registers its implementation via a simple Resolve<T>() call in OnStateChange — no IoC container, no reflection.
P0 Architectural Constraint ✓ One binary, zero code forks 4 interfaces: ICopySignalSource, IChartTraderBridge, IAtrSizingProvider, IPnlGate Disabled module = null provider = pass-through gate NT8-001/NT8-002 safe — plain C# interfaces only

What "adding a module" actually looks like in code
Phase 1 — today (~150 lines)
private void OnOrderUpdate(...)
{
  if (!_isCopyEnabled)     return;
  if (!IsCorrectSource(e)) return;
  if (!IsSubmitted(e))     return;
  if (IsDedup(e))          return;

  foreach (acc in _followers)
  {
    if (!PassesPnlCap(acc)) continue;
    SendCopy(acc, signal);
  }
}
Phase 2 — add license + tier (2 lines added)
private void OnOrderUpdate(...)
{
  if (!_isCopyEnabled)     return;
  if (!IsCorrectSource(e)) return;
  if (!IsSubmitted(e))     return;
  if (IsDedup(e))          return;
  if (!LicenseGate())      return; // ← insert

  foreach (acc in _followers)
  {
    if (!PassesPnlCap(acc))      continue;
    if (!TierAllows(acc, _tier)) continue; // ← insert
    SendCopy(acc, signal);
  }
}
The Phase 1 code is never rewritten. License and tier logic live in their own methods — isolated, testable, removable. Code delivered to customers can have the license gate compiled in but dormant until you flip a server-side flag.

B10 — Duplicate Panel + Menu Fix — DW-B10-DIAG-DUPLICATE-01 + DW-B10-DIAG-LEADER-01 ✓ COMPLETE — F5 gate PASSED
Bug: Each F5 recompile appends a second TradeCopierPanel row to ChartTrader's WPF Grid without first checking whether one is already there. NT8 reuses the ChartTrader Grid across NinjaScript domain reloads — the old panel survives, the new AddOn instance appends a second one on top of it.

Root cause: DoInject calls _panels.ContainsKey(chart) as its only guard. That dictionary tracks only the current AddOn instance — it has no knowledge of what's already in the WPF visual tree from a prior instance. After F5, _panels is an empty dictionary in the new domain, so ContainsKey always returns false and another row is added.

Not caused by the B9/B10 edits. All 5 .cs files have identical MD5 hashes between workspace and NT8 bin. This is a pre-existing structural gap triggered by every F5 compile.
Fix stepLocationChange
1 — Atomic slot claim DoInject entry Replace ContainsKey guard with _panels.TryAdd(chart, null). First caller wins the slot; all subsequent calls return immediately. Atomic — no race on parallel chart opens.
2 — Visual tree scan DoInject before grid.Children.Add Scan grid.Children for an existing TradeCopierPanel. If found → adopt it: call existing.SetInstrument() + existing.SetChart(), skip adding a new row. If not found → fresh inject as before (add RowDefinition + panel).
3 — Remove redundant guard InjectIntoChart L128 Remove the outer ContainsKey guard — TryAdd in step 1 is the single authority. 
// BEFORE (broken — ContainsKey only sees current AddOn instance)
if (_panels.ContainsKey(chart)) return;
grid.RowDefinitions.Add(new RowDefinition());
grid.Children.Add(panel);

// AFTER — Adopt-or-Inject pattern (CYC=7, single file: TradeCopierAddOn.cs)
if (!_panels.TryAdd(chart, null)) return;  // atomic slot claim

// Scan visual tree for survivor panel from prior AddOn instance
TradeCopierPanel existing = null;
foreach (UIElement child in grid.Children)
    if (child is TradeCopierPanel p) { existing = p; break; }

if (existing != null)
{
    existing.SetInstrument(chart.Instrument); // re-wire to new chart
    existing.SetChart(chart);
    _panels[chart] = existing;               // adopt — no new row added
    return;
}

// No survivor — fresh inject
var panel = new TradeCopierPanel(chart);
grid.RowDefinitions.Add(new RowDefinition { Height = GridLength.Auto });
Grid.SetRow(panel, grid.RowDefinitions.Count - 1);
grid.Children.Add(panel);
_panels[chart] = panel;
Only file changed: TradeCopierAddOn.cs — two methods (DoInject + remove guard from InjectIntoChart).
Hard link: AtrSizingEngine.cs hard link was broken by the prior session's write-then-revert. Content is correct but future workspace edits won't propagate until deploy-sync.ps1 is re-run.
New test: Call DoInject twice on same mock chart → assert grid.Children.Count == 1. CYC=7 (within ≤8).
Related gap — DW-B10-DIAG-LEADER-01 — SetLeaderAccount never wired
Gap: TradeCopierPanel.SetLeaderAccount() exists but is never called. _leaderAccount is always null. The ChartTrader Sim101 account dropdown is visible in the UI but its selection is never relayed to the panel. This blocks GAP-001d, click-trader buy/sell, and ApplyRule.

Proper fix (staged with DUPLICATE-01): In DoInject, after injecting the panel, walk the visual tree to find the ChartTrader account ComboBox, read its current SelectedItem as an Account, call panel.SetLeaderAccount() immediately, then subscribe to SelectionChanged to keep it live as the user switches accounts.

The diag doesn't need the configured leader; it just needs any account that has a working stop order. 
// BEFORE — OnDiagGap001d (broken: _leaderAccount always null)
if (_leaderAccount == null || _instrument == null) { /* bail */ return; }
TradeCopierAddOn.RunGap001dTest(_leaderAccount, _instrument);

// AFTER — Option A workaround (find Sim account from Account.All, same as GAP-002)
if (_instrument == null) { /* bail */ return; }
NinjaTrader.Cbi.Account diagAcc = null;
foreach (var a in NinjaTrader.Cbi.Account.All)
    if (a.Name.IndexOf("Sim", System.StringComparison.OrdinalIgnoreCase) >= 0)
        { diagAcc = a; break; }
if (diagAcc == null) { _statusText.Text = "GAP-001d: no Sim account found"; return; }
TradeCopierAddOn.RunGap001dTest(diagAcc, _instrument);
Scope: TradeCopierPanel.cs — OnDiagGap001d only. CYC unchanged (still 2). This workaround is diag-only — the proper SetLeaderAccount wiring fix ships with DUPLICATE-01 in the same workflow ticket.
NT8 AddOn Domain Reload Model — Permanent Knowledge Item
On every F5 compile NT8 unloads the current AddOn assembly and loads the newly compiled one. The consequences for AddOn authors:

1 — All static fields reset. static, static readonly, and static volatile fields re-initialize to their declared defaults on every reload. Any dictionary, flag, or cache that tracked prior state is empty/false in the new domain. Never use static field state as a re-entry guard across F5.

2 — NT8 WPF surfaces persist. The ChartTrader Grid, the Control Center menu, and all other NT8 WPF windows survive reloads. Any element your old AddOn appended to those surfaces is still there. NT8 re-fires OnWindowCreated for existing windows after reload so the new instance can re-wire.

3 — CLR type identity breaks across domains. child is TradeCopierPanel and child as TradeCopierPanel return false / null for instances created by the old domain. Use child.GetType().Name == "TradeCopierPanel" for cross-domain identity checks. Similarly, item as NTMenuItem can fail for items whose runtime type is System.Windows.Controls.MenuItem — cast to the WPF base class instead.

4 — NT8 types are never reloaded. NTMenuItem, ChartTrader, etc. live in NinjaTrader.Gui.dll which is never reloaded. Casts to these types always succeed regardless of which AddOn domain created the object.

Correct pattern for any AddOn surface that persists across F5: scan the live NT8 surface (visual tree / menu Items collection) unconditionally on each OnWindowCreated, purge all stale AddOn-owned elements (and their RowDefinitions), then inject fresh. Do not rely on static flags or dictionaries as duplicate guards.
Patch 3 — why Patch 2 still duplicated (and the fix that worked)
Menu (Patch 2 failure): The survivor scan used existing as NTMenuItem. NT8’s built-in “New” submenu items have runtime type System.Windows.Controls.MenuItem, not NTMenuItem. Every item cast returned null; the loop skipped everything; the fresh-add path always ran → second entry appended.

Patch 3 fix — remove-then-add: Reverse-index loop casts to System.Windows.Controls.MenuItem (WPF base — catches every subtype including NTMenuItem). Removes any item with Header == "Trade Copier", then unconditionally appends a fresh entry with the current domain’s delegate. 
// WireControlCenterMenu — Patch 3
for (int i = newMenu.Items.Count - 1; i >= 0; i--)
{
    var mi = newMenu.Items[i] as System.Windows.Controls.MenuItem; // WPF base
    if (mi == null) continue;
    if (mi.Header != null && mi.Header.ToString() == "Trade Copier")
        newMenu.Items.RemoveAt(i);
}
var entry = new NTMenuItem { Header = "Trade Copier" };
entry.Click += OnMenuItemClick;
newMenu.Items.Add(entry);
Panel (Patch 2 failure): Patch 2 removed the stale panel from grid.Children but did not remove the RowDefinition that was added for it. The fresh-inject path then appended a new RowDefinition + new panel. After N reloads: N empty rows + 1 live panel row — visually N duplicate rows.

Patch 3 fix — collect-purge-then-inject: Collect all stale children into a List<UIElement> first (cannot remove from UIElementCollection during enumeration), then for each: remove from grid.Children AND call grid.RowDefinitions.RemoveAt(staleRow) if staleRow > 0 (guards NT8’s own rows). Falls through to existing fresh-inject path. 
// DoInject — Patch 3 purge block (before fresh-inject path)
var stale = new System.Collections.Generic.List<UIElement>();
foreach (UIElement child in grid.Children)
    if (child.GetType().Name == "TradeCopierPanel")   // string match — cross-domain safe
        stale.Add(child);
foreach (var old in stale)
{
    int staleRow = System.Windows.Controls.Grid.GetRow(old);
    grid.Children.Remove(old);
    if (staleRow > 0 && staleRow < grid.RowDefinitions.Count)
        grid.RowDefinitions.RemoveAt(staleRow);   // ← removes accumulated RowDefinition too
}

✓ F5 gate PASSED ×3 TradeCopierAddOn.cs only DoInject + WireControlCenterMenu WireLeaderAccount live T-B10-REPAIR-01 passing deploy-sync.ps1 PASS
B11 — ATR-Based Live Stop Sync — DW-B11-ATR-STOP-SYNC-01 Deferred
Feature: When the live ATR changes (on each bar close), automatically update the pending (Working) stop orders on both leader and follower accounts to reflect the new ATR-derived stop distance. Keeps all account stops correctly sized as volatility shifts — no manual stop adjustment needed.

Prerequisite: B10 T4 must resolve chart.NinjaScripts.Add API so AtrSizingEngine has real bar data and a live ATR value.
QuestionDecisionRationale
Which order types? StopMarket + StopLimit only — fixed stops only Profit target (Limit) never touched — R:R is sacrosanct. Trailing stops (TrailPrice > 0) skipped — GAP-001 caveat applies.
Trigger Bar close — AtrSizingEngine.OnBarUpdate ATR changes on bar close. Updating every tick would spam acc.Change() when ATR barely changed. Bar close aligns update cadence with ATR calculation cadence.
NT8 API order.StopPrice = newStop; acc.Change(new Order[] { order }) Same pattern used by HandleBracketChange and MoveStopToBreakEven. No new API needed.
Does leader stop relay to followers via OrderUpdate? NO — update followers independently OnOrderUpdate only copies at OrderState.Submitted. A Working→Working modification is NOT a new submission — the copier gate chain rejects it. Leader and followers are updated in the same SyncAtrStops() loop, not through the relay path.
What about profit target when stop moves? Nothing — target unaffected acc.Change() on the stop order only affects the stop order. Profit target is a separate Limit order. ATM session continues unchanged.
Feature flag? EnableAtrStopSync bool on CopyRule — default OFF Changes live orders automatically — must be explicit opt-in. Independent of EnableAtrSizing. Both can be toggled independently per rule. 
// New CopyRule fields (B11)
public bool   EnableAtrStopSync   = false;  // default OFF — explicit opt-in
public int    AtrStopSyncMinTicks = 1;      // skip update if change < 1 tick (noise filter)
public double AtrStopFraction    = 1.0;    // stop = ceil(ATR * fraction), default 1x ATR

// New CopyEngine.SyncAtrStops() — called by AtrSizingEngine.OnBarUpdate
// CYC outer = 5  (guards + loop body extracted into ApplyAtrStopToAccount CYC=4)
internal void SyncAtrStops(CopyRule rule, Instrument instrument, double newAtrPoints)
{
    // stopDist = ceil( atrPoints * fraction / tick ) * tick  -- whole-tick stop distance
    // foreach acc in AllAccounts: find working fixed stop, apply delta guard,
    // safety check (don't move past current price), then acc.Change()
    // Skip: trailing stops, flat accounts, no working stop, change < MinTicks
}

// Trigger in AtrSizingEngine.OnBarUpdate (after ApplyResize):
CopyEngine.Instance?.SyncAtrStops(_rule, Instrument, _lastAtr);

// Price safety guard (never trigger the stop by moving it):
bool wouldTrigger = isLong
    ? newStop >= instrument.MarketData.Bid.Price
    : newStop <= instrument.MarketData.Ask.Price;
if (wouldTrigger) continue; // skip — would execute stop immediately
DW-B11-ATR-STOP-SYNC-01 Prerequisite: B10 T4 chart attachment Default OFF — explicit opt-in per rule CYC=5 outer + CYC=4 inner helper No Sim101 gate needed — proven APIs only
ATR-Based Dynamic Contract Sizing — B9 (P1) ✓ COMPLETE
Architecture note: MarketData.Subscribe and bar data access are only available on NinjaScriptBase subclasses (Strategy, Indicator). AddOnBase has no OnBarUpdate, no Data[] series, no MarketData property. B9 design: AtrSizingEngine.cs as a detached Indicator managed by the AddOn.

Core rule: ATR up → contracts down. ATR down → contracts up. Every trade maintains fixed max-risk-per-trade in dollars regardless of volatility.
◯ PENDING WORKFLOW — Two-Ceiling Formula Upgrade
// Formula CURRENT (Math.Floor, no fraction) — clean baseline
contracts = floor( max_risk_dollars / (atr_points * tick_dollar_value) )

// Formula TARGET (Two-Ceiling + atrFraction) — pending workflow execution
// Proved against Scotty's live tool: /ES ATR=3.43, fraction=0.75
//   stopPoints = ceil(3.43 * 0.75) = ceil(2.57) = 3 pts
//   riskPerCont = 3pts * $50/pt = $150
//   contracts   = ceil($200 / $150) = ceil(1.33) = 2 contracts  ✓
stopPoints  = ceil( ATR(14) * atrFraction )       // ceil 1: whole-point stop
riskPerCont = stopPoints * tickDollarValue
contracts   = ceil( maxRisk / riskPerCont )       // ceil 2: qty — never under-size

// Example: MES, max_risk=$150, tick=$1.25, point=$5, atrFraction=1.0
ATR  6pts  ->  stop=6pts, risk/c=$30  ->  ceil(5.0)=5 contracts
ATR  8pts  ->  stop=8pts, risk/c=$40  ->  ceil(3.75)=4 contracts
ATR 12pts  ->  stop=12pts, risk/c=$60  ->  ceil(2.5)=3 contracts

// Src change required: AtrSizingEngine.cs::CalcContracts(atrPoints, maxRisk, tickDollarValue)
// Add atrFraction param. Replace Math.Floor with two Math.Ceiling calls above.
// Staged for ptt-orchestrator workflow. Do NOT edit .cs directly.
New file: AtrSizingEngine.cs (detached Indicator host) Uses existing order.Change() API CYC<=8: CalcAtr() + CalcContracts() + ApplyResize()
Click Trader (Chart-Click Limit Entry) — B9 (P1)
Tap a price level on the chart → limit order placed at that price on the master account → CopyEngine copies to all followers as normal. Visual mode indicator: green border around chart when click-trading is armed (same pattern as aiEnhancedChartTrader and V12). ATM template: NT native (user selects before arming). Qty: AtrSizingEngine if enabled.
In scope (bare minimum)
  • ChartControl.MouseDown → GetValueByY() → price

Out of scope (deferred)

Two Copy Modes (Mirror vs Signal) — B9 (P2)
MODE 1 — Signal (current behavior)
Copy entry order only.
Follower ATM template manages their own stops + targets independently.
Each follower can have a different ATM / risk profile.
✓ Already built
MODE 2 — Mirror (future)

Master moves stop → all follower stops move (auto BreakEven).
Master closes → all followers close (auto Flatten).
◯ B9 candidate
