Q: Can PTT replicate NT8's buttons? Can the panel collapse? — Architecture Analysis
Q1: Can PTT put a copy of NT8's Buy/Sell/Account/ATM controls in our panel?
What Affordable Indicators does: Their panel is a separate window placed to the left of the standard ChartTrader column — not injected into it. It replicates NT8's Buy/Sell/Account/ATM controls entirely using their own WPF buttons wired to the same Account.CreateOrder() path. This allows traders to hide the standard ChartTrader column entirely and use only the custom panel for everything.

PTT's current architecture is different: we inject one row into the bottom of NT8's ChartTrader Grid — we augment it, we don't replace it.
NT8 ChartTrader control Can PTT replicate it? Mechanism Verdict
Buy Market / Sell Market buttons ✓ Yes Our own WPF Button calls Account.CreateOrder() — the same internal path NT8's buttons use. Already proven via click-trader (B9). Style via NTButtonStyle resource key. Done in B9 (click-trader)
Buy Bid / Sell Ask (limit at best price) ✓ Yes Same as Market but with OrderType.Limit + Instrument.MarketData.Bid/Ask as the price. Already the mechanism PTT Flatten uses. Feasible — OOS for now
Account selector dropdown ✓ Yes WPF ComboBox bound to Account.All — same list NT8 shows. We already walk the visual tree to READ ChartTrader's account selector. Writing our own is simpler: just bind to Account.All. Feasible — OOS for now
ATM strategy selector dropdown ✓ Yes WPF ComboBox bound to NinjaTrader.NinjaScript.AtmStrategy.AtmStrategyTemplates (decompiled: static collection). Already referenced in B11 ATM template writer work (cbxStrategySelector visual tree walk). Feasible — OOS for now
Quantity box ✓ Yes QtyControl.Value write confirmed in B10 investigation. PTT already writes to this control. We can also render our own TextBox bound to the same value. Confirmed B10
Close Position button (NT8 native) ⚠ Partially NT8's Close button is a market order to the single ChartTrader account (decompiled proof, B9). PTT Flatten sends limit orders to all follower accounts simultaneously — strictly more capable. Replicating NT8's exact Close is pointless; PTT Flatten replaces it. PTT Flatten is better
Current PTT approach: inject one row
✓ Zero window chrome — no resize, drag, or positioning for the user to manage.
✓ Lives exactly where the user already looks (below Buy/Sell).
✓ Can't get lost or minimised behind other windows.
✗ NT8's own rows always visible above ours — can't hide them.
✗ Panel height = ChartTrader row height — fixed by NT8's layout.
Affordable Indicators approach: side-by-side window
✓ Can replicate and replace all NT8 native controls.
✓ Collapsible sections stack vertically — unlimited height.
✓ User can hide NT8's standard column → single-panel workflow.
✗ Requires the user to position/resize the window on first setup.
✗ Not injected — can be accidentally closed or hidden.
Spec decision: PTT keeps the injected-row approach as primary. This is the correct choice for a copier + action buttons product — the user needs Buy/Sell in one click, not two windows. The side-by-side approach is worth speccing as an optional "full-screen mode" (B12+ scope) for traders who want to hide the standard ChartTrader column entirely. This would be a separate TradeCopierFullPanel UserControl that replaces the ChartTrader column rather than augmenting it. Not a B11 concern.
✓ Buy/Sell: feasible via Account.CreateOrder() — already done in B9 Account/ATM/Qty controls: all feasible — OOS B12+ Full-panel mode (hide NT8 column): B12+ scope, optional
Q2: Can PTT's injected panel be collapsible/expandable? Can NT8's own rows collapse too?
Two separate sub-questions with different technical constraints:
✓ OUR INJECTED ROWS — can collapse/expand
Our row is a standard WPF RowDefinition in NT8's ChartTrader Grid. We own its Height property completely. Setting it to GridLength.Auto means the row height = the content height. Setting our panel's content Visibility = Collapsed collapses the row to zero height. A chevron [∨] / [∧] button toggles it. NT8's own rows are unaffected. 
// In our injected panel — collapse button handler
// CYC=2 (toggle state check)
void OnCollapseToggle()
{
    _isCollapsed = !_isCollapsed;
    _contentPanel.Visibility = _isCollapsed
        ? Visibility.Collapsed    // row shrinks to 0
        : Visibility.Visible;     // row expands to content height
    _chevron.Content = _isCollapsed ? "▶" : "▼";
}
// RowDefinition.Height = Auto — size follows content.
// No explicit height management needed.
The collapsed state shows only a slim header bar (1 row, ~24px): [▶ PTT] [COPY ON] — copy toggle always visible even when collapsed.
✗ NT8'S OWN ROWS — cannot collapse them
NT8's ChartTrader rows (Buy/Sell, Account selector, ATM selector, Qty box, Close button) are managed by NT8 internally. Their RowDefinition heights are set by NT8's layout code — they are not GridLength.Auto. We can read their heights; we cannot reliably write to them without risking NT8's layout engine breaking on next redraw.
Why not? The ChartTrader Grid is a live WPF object owned by NT8. Mutating its RowDefinition heights from an AddOn is an unsupported operation. NT8 can — and does — recalculate layout on any account switch, instrument change, or resize event, restoring its own row heights. Any collapse we applied would be undone.
The only way to hide NT8's own rows is the side-by-side approach (Affordable Indicators model) — place PTT as a separate window to the left, and let the user manually collapse or hide the standard ChartTrader column in NT8's layout settings. Not PTT's responsibility.
PTT collapsible panel design — what the user sees
EXPANDED (default)
─── NT8: Account ─── ATM ─── Qty ─── Buy/Sell ───
▼ PTT                              [COPY ON   ]
[Trim ½] [Flatten] [Cancel] [BE +2tks]
[Signal ◉] [Mirror ○]   Followers: 3 ✓
COLLAPSED
─── NT8: Account ─── ATM ─── Qty ─── Buy/Sell ───
▶ PTT                              [COPY ON   ]
(click ▶ to expand)
Copy toggle always visible.
Row height ≈ 28px (header bar only).
NT8's rows are completely unaffected.
NT8 implementation note: Set the injected RowDefinition.Height = GridLength.Auto at injection time (already done per current spec at line ~2653). When _contentPanel.Visibility = Collapsed, the Auto row height becomes 0 automatically — no manual height calculation needed. The header bar (chevron + copy toggle) lives in a separate always-visible child above the content panel. volatile bool _isCollapsed (JS-023) stores state. Persist to copy_rules.xml so the panel remembers its state across NT8 restarts (same serialization as B6 persistence).
Ticket stub — DW-B12-COLLAPSE-01
FileTradeCopierPanel.cs ChangeAdd header bar child (always visible). Wrap existing content in _contentPanel StackPanel. Add chevron Button + OnCollapseToggle(). Persist IsCollapsed to XML. CYC+2 (toggle handler) — all other CYC unchanged PrerequisitesNone — pure WPF visibility change, no NT8 API dependency PriorityP3 — UX polish. B12 scope. Unblocked now.
✓ Our rows: collapsible via Visibility.Collapsed + GridLength.Auto ✗ NT8's own rows: cannot collapse — NT8 layout engine re-asserts them Copy toggle always visible even when panel collapsed DW-B12-COLLAPSE-01 — P3, unblocked, ~+2 CYC Persist collapsed state to copy_rules.xml (B6 pattern)
Q3: Would PTT's replicated buttons be identical to NT8's native buttons — or mimicry?
SHORT ANSWER — from decompiled source
Our buttons would call Account.CreateOrder() directly — the same NT8 API that NT8's own buttons call internally. The order that reaches the broker is byte-for-byte identical to what NT8's native button produces.

One critical difference: NT8's ChartTrader button goes through ChartTrader.SubmitOrder() first — an internal static method that is AgileDotNet-obfuscated and inaccessible to AddOns. That method handles ATM bracket attachment — it reads cbxStrategySelector.SelectedValue and wires the ATM strategy to the entry order atomically before handing off to Account.CreateOrder(). PTT's direct Account.CreateOrder() call skips this step — no ATM bracket attaches automatically.
Call chain comparison — NT8 native button vs PTT replicated button
NT8 native Buy Market button
1. btnBuyMkt.Click → WPF routed event
↓
2. ChartTrader.OnSubmitOrder()
↓
3. ChartTrader.SubmitOrder() ← SEALED
reads cbxStrategySelector → attaches ATM
reads this.account (private field)
AgileDotNet obfuscated — AddOn cannot reach
↓
4. account.CreateOrder() ← public API
order reaches broker with ATM bracket attached
PTT replicated Buy Market button
1. PTT button.Click → WPF routed event
↓
2. PTT.OnBuyClick() — our handler
reads _leaderAccount (from visual tree walk)
reads ChartTrader.Instrument
↓
3. ⚠ ATM STEP MISSING
ChartTrader.SubmitOrder() is sealed/obfuscated.
We skip straight to CreateOrder().
No ATM bracket attaches.
↓
4. account.CreateOrder() ← same public API
naked entry order — no bracket
What this means in practice — the three cases
Control PTT = identical to NT8? What's different Workaround available?
Buy Market / Sell Market
no ATM, pure market entry		 ✓ Identical at the broker Same Account.CreateOrder(OrderType.Market) call. Order that hits the exchange is bit-for-bit the same. N/A — already identical
Buy Market / Sell Market
with ATM selected in ChartTrader		 ✗ NOT identical NT8's button attaches the ATM bracket atomically. PTT's button sends a naked entry. No stop, no target, no trail. ⚠ Yes — write ATM template first (Path 2, B11 spec), then pass signalName in CreateOrder() matching the template name. NT8 auto-attaches ATM by signal name match.
Buy Bid / Sell Ask
limit at best price		 ✓ Identical at the broker Same Account.CreateOrder(OrderType.Limit, bid/ask). PTT Flatten already uses this path — proven working. N/A — already identical
Close Position (NT8 native) ⚠ Different — but PTT is better NT8 Close = market order, 1 account. PTT Flatten = limit@bid/ask, N accounts simultaneously. PTT Flatten replaces this — strictly superior
ATM workaround — signalName matching (the same mechanism PTT already uses for follower ATM dispatch)
NT8's ATM system attaches a bracket to an entry by matching the signalName parameter in Account.CreateOrder() against the selected ATM template name. If the names match, NT8 auto-attaches the bracket. This is the same mechanism PTT already uses for follower ATM dispatch (B8, FollowerAtmMode.Named). 
// NT8 native button path (what SubmitOrder does internally — inferred from ATM behaviour):
// signalName = selected ATM template name → NT8 auto-attaches the bracket on fill
account.CreateOrder(instrument, action, OrderType.Market, OrderEntry.Manual,
    TimeInForce.Day, qty, 0, 0,
    null,
    "MES-RMA-6SL",  // ← signalName = ATM template name → auto-attach
    DateTime.MaxValue, null);

// PTT replicated button — to match NT8 exactly:
// 1. Read selected template from ChartTrader's cbxStrategySelector (visual tree walk — already done in B11)
// 2. Pass that name as signalName in CreateOrder
string atmName = GetSelectedAtmTemplateName();  // visual tree walk, already specced B11
account.CreateOrder(instrument, action, OrderType.Market, OrderEntry.Manual,
    TimeInForce.Day, qty, 0, 0,
    null,
    atmName,  // ← ATM auto-attaches — identical to NT8 native button
    DateTime.MaxValue, null);
Result: With this workaround the order that reaches the broker is identical to clicking NT8's native Buy Market button with an ATM selected. The signalName mechanism is the same thing PTT already does for follower Named ATM dispatch — PTT has all the infrastructure. This is not specced for immediate execution (B12+ scope when full-panel mode is built) but the mechanism is confirmed and documented here.
Verdict: PTT's replicated buttons are not mimicry — they call the same underlying NT8 API (Account.CreateOrder()) that NT8 itself uses. The order that hits the broker is identical. The one gap is ATM bracket attachment — NT8 handles this in a sealed method we cannot call. The gap is closable via the signalName workaround (read selected template from ChartTrader's dropdown, pass it as the signal name — NT8 auto-attaches the bracket). PTT already uses this exact mechanism for follower Named ATM dispatch (B8).

Architectural summary: NT8's button → sealed handler → CreateOrder(). PTT's button → our handler → CreateOrder(). Same destination. One layer fewer indirection. The ATM step is the only thing that lives in the sealed layer — and it is bypassable via signalName.
✓ Same API — Account.CreateOrder() — identical order at broker ✗ ATM bracket: sealed in ChartTrader.SubmitOrder() (obfuscated) ⚠ ATM gap closable: signalName = template name → NT8 auto-attaches PTT already uses signalName for follower Named ATM (B8) Full-panel mode (B12+) can be fully equivalent to NT8 native buttons
▶ Locked Design Principle: Use NT8 infrastructure as-is. PTT only changes numbers.
THE RULE — ALL BLOCKS, ALL TIME
PTT never modifies NT8's order infrastructure, ATM bracket format, or order management logic. NT8's ATM template system, bracket structure, trail logic, and OCO linking are owned entirely by NT8 — PTT treats them as a black box.

The only things PTT ever changes are numbers: qty, stopPrice, limitPrice, and entryQty in the ATM template XML before NT8 reads it. Everything else — bracket structure, trail behaviour, profit target, OCO logic — is NT8's. PTT never re-implements what NT8 already does.
Complete PTT button inventory — NT8 API used + ATM gap status
PTT Button / Action NT8 API called PTT changes ATM gap?
Copy ON/OFF toggle no order — engine flag only _isCopyEnabled bool ✓ No gap — no order
Trim ½ acc.CreateOrder(Limit, bid/ask) qty = ceil(pos/2), price = bid or ask ✓ No gap — exit order, no ATM needed. NT8 ATM stops remain untouched.
Flatten acc.CreateOrder(Limit, bid/ask) qty = full pos qty, price = bid or ask ✓ No gap — exit order, NT8 ATM OCO handles bracket cancellation natively on fill.
Flatten ⚡ Panic acc.Flatten(Instrument[]) nothing — NT8 owns the entire call ✓ No gap — this IS NT8's own method, not a CreateOrder call.
Cancel acc.Cancel(Order[]) nothing — NT8 cancels the order ✓ No gap — cancelling an order, not creating one.
Break Even acc.Change(Order[]) order.StopPrice = entryPrice + bufferTicks ✓ No gap — modifying a stop price on an existing order. ATM trail continues from the new price.
ATR stop sync acc.Change(Order[]) order.StopPrice = newAtrStop ✓ No gap — same as BE: number change on existing order. NT8 ATM bracket format unchanged.
ATR template sizing File write to PTT_ATR_LIVE.xml before NT8 reads it StopLoss ticks + EntryQuantity in XML ✓ No gap — PTT writes two numbers into the template XML. NT8 reads the template and manages all bracket logic itself. PTT never touches bracket structure.
Click Trader [Buy] / [Sell]
(B9 — leader entry via chart click)		 leaderAccount.CreateOrder(Market) qty, price (from click Y coord) ⚠ Gap exists — creates a new entry. If an ATM is selected in ChartTrader, NT8 won't attach it automatically.
Fix: read cbxStrategySelector.SelectedValue from visual tree and pass it as signalName. Then NT8 attaches the ATM bracket exactly as it would for a native button click.
Copy — follower entry
(CopyEngine on OrderUpdate)		 follower.CreateOrder() × N qty × multiplier, price from leader order ✓ No gap — followers use their own ATM template (Named mode, B8). The signalName mechanism is already used here. NT8 attaches each follower's bracket per their own template.
[Buy Market] / [Sell Market]
(B12+ full-panel replicated buttons)		 leaderAccount.CreateOrder(Market) qty ⚠ Gap exists — same as Click Trader. Fix is identical: read cbxStrategySelector.SelectedValue → pass as signalName. One line. NT8 owns the rest.
Gap scope — exactly two buttons, exactly one fix, both already have the fix mechanism
BUTTONS WITH THE ATM GAP
1. Click Trader [Buy]/[Sell] (B9) — creates a new leader entry
2. Replicated [Buy]/[Sell] (B12+) — creates a new leader entry

Both are entry-creating buttons for the leader account only.
All other PTT buttons (Trim, Flatten, Cancel, BE, ATR sync) modify or cancel existing orders — no ATM attachment needed.
THE FIX — ONE LINE, ALREADY IN THE CODEBASE
// Before CreateOrder(), read ChartTrader's selected ATM:
string atmName = GetSelectedAtmTemplateName();
// (visual tree walk to cbxStrategySelector — already done B11)

// Pass it as signalName — NT8 attaches bracket on fill:
acc.CreateOrder(..., atmName, ...);
NT8 owns all bracket logic after this. PTT hands off completely. No bracket format knowledge needed.
Principle confirmed: PTT is correct by construction on this. Every existing PTT button already follows the rule — Trim, Flatten, Cancel, BE, and ATR sync all modify or cancel existing orders via NT8's own APIs (acc.Change, acc.Cancel, acc.Flatten), changing only the numbers the user needs changed. The ATM gap only applies to the two entry-creating buttons (Click Trader and the future replicated buttons). For those, the fix is one line — pass the selected ATM template name as signalName — and NT8 does everything else. PTT never needs to know what's inside the bracket. That's NT8's business.
✓ 9 of 11 PTT actions: no ATM gap — modify/cancel existing orders only ⚠ 2 of 11: entry-creating buttons — ATM gap exists, 1-line fix via signalName ✓ All existing B1–B11 buttons: already gap-free Gap only opens when B12+ full-panel replicated buttons are built Click Trader (B9) fix: read cbxStrategySelector → pass as signalName
Scalper Trade Lifecycle — Every Exit Path
Every trade is either trim → flatten, flatten, target hit, or stop hit. The BE Armed button covers the case where price moves against you before the ATM trail/BE triggers. Green paths = NT8 owns entirely. Blue paths = PTT buttons act. Red path = panic, market order.
Full Trade State Machine
flowchart TD ENTER(["⬤ ENTER\nposition opens"]):::entry ATM["ATM bracket activates\nStop + Target working"]:::nt8 ENTER --> ATM ATM --> A_dir["price moves IN YOUR DIRECTION"]:::node ATM --> C_dir["price moves AGAINST YOU"]:::node A_dir --> A_target["reaches ATM target"]:::nt8 A_dir --> A_notarget["doesn't reach ATM trigger\nbefore you decide to exit"]:::node A_target --> A_done(["✓ Target filled\nNT8 closes position"]):::nt8done A_notarget --> TRIM["TRIM ½\nLimit @ bid/ask\nPTT button"]:::ptt A_notarget --> FLATTEN_FULL["FLATTEN\nLimit @ bid/ask\nPTT button"]:::ptt TRIM --> after_trim{"remainder"} after_trim --> FLATTEN_REM["FLATTEN remainder\nLimit @ bid/ask\nPTT button"]:::ptt after_trim --> ATM_TAKES["wait — ATM\ntakes remainder"]:::nt8 FLATTEN_FULL --> DONE_B(["✓ Flat"]):::done FLATTEN_REM --> DONE_B2(["✓ Flat"]):::done ATM_TAKES --> DONE_ATM(["✓ ATM exit"]):::nt8done C_dir --> BE_ARM["BE ARMED button\nwatches price"]:::ptt BE_ARM --> BE_MOVE["price reaches entry\nstop moved to BE\nacc.Change()"]:::ptt BE_MOVE --> C_stop(["✓ stopped out at BE\nNT8 handles"]):::nt8done BE_MOVE --> C_recover["price recovers"]:::nt8 C_recover --> ATM_TAKES2["ATM takes over again"]:::nt8 ATM_TAKES2 --> DONE_ATM2(["✓ ATM exit"]):::nt8done C_dir --> D_hard["hard reversal\nstop not enough"]:::danger D_hard --> PANIC["FLATTEN ⚡\nmarket order\nacc.Flatten()\nONE TAP — no confirm"]:::panic PANIC --> DONE_D(["✓ Flat — out now"]):::done classDef entry fill:#1c2133,stroke:#2563eb,color:#f1f5f9,font-weight:700 classDef nt8 fill:#111520,stroke:#4b5563,color:#9ca3af classDef nt8done fill:#052e16,stroke:#22c55e,color:#22c55e,font-weight:700 classDef ptt fill:#1e3a5f,stroke:#2563eb,color:#93c5fd,font-weight:600 classDef done fill:#0b1a0b,stroke:#22c55e,color:#22c55e classDef danger fill:#1a0b0b,stroke:#ef4444,color:#fca5a5 classDef panic fill:#3b0000,stroke:#ef4444,color:#ef4444,font-weight:700 classDef node fill:#111520,stroke:#1c2133,color:#9ca3af
Ownership — NT8 vs PTT per scenario
Scenario Trigger Owner Order type PTT button
A — Target hit ATM target order fills NT8 entirely Limit (ATM) None — PTT untouched
A — Stop hit ATM stop order fills NT8 entirely StopMarket (ATM) None — PTT untouched
B — Trim + Flatten Manually scale out, then close remainder PTT Limit @ bid/ask Trim ½ → Flatten
B — Full Flatten Close full position manually PTT Limit @ bid/ask Flatten
C — BE Armed Price moves against — arm pending BE PTT → NT8 acc.Change() — no new order BE Armed
C — Stopped out at BE Stop at BE fills NT8 StopMarket (ATM) None
C — Recovers, ATM takes over Price returns, ATM bracket still working NT8 entirely ATM handles None
D — Hard reversal panic Runaway loss, get out now PTT Market — acc.Flatten() — guaranteed Flatten ⚡ (panic)

Trim ½ & Flatten — Limit @ bid/ask
Both use OrderType.Limit at the current bid (if long) or ask (if short).
Price read from new MarketData(instrument).Bid.Price at click time — no persistent subscription.
On liquid futures (ES, NQ) during RTH: fills in <500ms essentially always.
Collects the spread instead of paying it — free ticks every exit.
NT8 Close button = market (proven from decompiled source).
We are strictly better than NT8's own Close button.
Flatten ⚡ — Market · Panic Only
Calls account.Flatten(new[] { instrument }) — NT8's own guaranteed market exit.
One tap. No confirmation dialog. No toggle.
Visually distinct: dark red background, amber ⚡ icon, 2px red border.
Physically separated from normal Flatten by a divider — can't accidentally hit it.
Used only for Scenario D: hard reversal where limit won't fill fast enough.
NT8 source: Position.Close() and Account.Flatten() state machines have no limitPrice field — they are market-only by architecture.
NT8 SOURCE PROOF — ChartTrader btnClose → OnClose() → Position.Close(). Decompiled state machine <Close>d__15 fields: orderAction, quantity — no limitPrice, no stopPrice anywhere in the chain. Account.Positions2Close helper class Hxg= fields: Order, int, int, bool — no price. NT8's Close is always market. Our Flatten (Limit@bid/ask) is strictly better for normal exits.
NT8 Native vs PTT Buttons — Competitive Matrix & Hotkey Analysis
Every PTT button vs its closest NT8 native equivalent
PTT Button NT8 Native Equivalent PTT Advantage
ATR sizing Manual ATM template swap Auto-calculates stop ticks + qty from live ATR every bar. Zero manual swaps.
Flatten ⚡ (panic) Control Center → "Flatten All" (all instruments) or ChartTrader Close button (market) Scoped to this instrument only. Fires across all follower accounts in one tap. NT8's "Flatten All" nukes every open position on every instrument.
Flatten (normal) Buy Bid / Sell Ask buttons (1 account) — or Control Center 1 account at a time All follower accounts simultaneously. Limit@bid/ask = same free-tick benefit as NT8's Buy Bid/Sell Ask. NT8's Close button is market-only (decompiled proof).
Trim ½ Buy Bid / Sell Ask buttons (manual qty, 1 account) — or Control Center Ceiling(N/2) math computed automatically. All follower accounts in one tap. Limit@bid/ask = free ticks. NT8 has no "half-position" concept.
Cancel Control Center (manual, per-account, cancels all order types) Entry-orders only (bracket legs are protected by 3 independent guards). All follower accounts. 1 tap.
BE (break even) Manual drag stop on chart (1 account) acc.Change() to entry price + live-editable tick buffer. All follower accounts. 1 tap.
✓ Trim + Normal Flatten = Free Ticks — Confirmed
NT8's ChartTrader Buy Bid / Sell Ask buttons submit a Limit order at the current bid or ask price — the same mechanism PTT uses for Trim ½ and normal Flatten.

If Long → exit via Sell Limit at Bid. You become the bid; buyers already sitting there fill you immediately. You do NOT cross the spread — you collect it.
If Short → exit via Buy Limit at Ask. Same logic inverted.

Versus a market order: market sell hits the bid AND potentially slips 1–2 extra ticks in fast markets. On MES (tick = $1.25), even 1 saved tick per contract × 10 contracts = $12.50 on a Trim. Over 100 trades = $1,250 saved in spread friction. On NQ (tick = $5.00) the benefit is 4× larger.

One risk: if market gaps away before fill, the limit order sits unfilled. This is why Flatten ⚡ (panic path) always uses market — speed guarantee overrides tick savings in a runaway scenario.
⚠ SPEC CORRECTION — Confirmed KB Finding (B4, NT8_ADDON_KNOWLEDGE.md line 133)
NT8 WPF host rejects ALL Shift+letter KeyGesture combos at runtime.
This was confirmed during B4 development and documented in the NT8 hard constraints table. All previous spec references to Shift+T, Shift+F, Shift+C, Shift+B as working keyboard shortcuts are ASPIRATIONAL ONLY — they do not fire at runtime. The buttons they describe work perfectly via mouse click. The keyboard layer is broken for Shift+letter in NT8's WPF host. This section replaces those references with the full architecture decision.
All 8 Keyboard Mechanisms — Complete Evaluation
Mechanism Status How it works / limitation Verdict
① Shift+letter KeyBinding
(current spec)		 BROKEN WPF KeyGesture with Shift+letter is silently rejected by NT8's WPF host at runtime. NT8 intercepts these at the application level for its own order-entry shortcuts (B=Buy, S=Sell, etc.). Compiles. F5 passes. Does nothing when pressed. ❌ ELIMINATED — confirmed broken B4. Never use KeyBinding with letter keys in NT8.
② NT8 Tools → Keyboard
(built-in system)		 N/A NT8's hotkey config dialog. Maps built-in NT8 actions only (Close, Flatten All, Reverse, etc.). No public API for AddOns to register custom actions. Cannot inject "PTT Trim" as a named action in this system. ❌ NOT AVAILABLE for custom actions. Cannot use for our buttons.
③ WPF KeyBinding — non-letter combos
(Ctrl+1, Ctrl+F1 etc.)		 UNTESTED NT8 only intercepts Shift+letter combos. Non-letter modifier combos (Ctrl+1, Ctrl+Shift+1, Alt+F1 etc.) may pass through to our KeyBinding if NT8 doesn't have them mapped internally. Collision risk exists with some Ctrl+letter combos (Ctrl+Z = undo etc.). ⚠ POSSIBLE — needs Sim101 test DW-B11-HK-01 to confirm specific combos. Lower-risk keys: Ctrl+Shift+number or Alt+number rows.
④ WPF PreviewKeyDown on our panel
(tunnel phase)		 UNTESTED WPF keyboard routing: events tunnel DOWN (PreviewKey) before bubbling UP. KeyBinding uses the bubble phase — NT8 may consume keys before they bubble. PreviewKeyDown fires on ancestors BEFORE the target element. Hooking PreviewKeyDown on our RowPanel may intercept before NT8's bubble-phase handler. But if NT8 handles in tunnel phase too, still blocked. ⚠ POSSIBLE — different routing phase from KeyBinding. Worth testing. May still be blocked if NT8 uses tunnel phase for its shortcuts.
⑤ Chart Window PreviewKeyDown
(most promising)		 UNTESTED We already hold a reference to the Chart Window (gained during injection). Hook chart.PreviewKeyDown at the Window level. Fires for ALL keyboard input anywhere in that window, regardless of which element has focus. Use double-modifier (Ctrl+Shift+key) to minimise collision risk. Unhook in our cleanup/dispose. ★ BEST CANDIDATE — fires regardless of focus location within chart window. Needs Sim101 test DW-B11-HK-02. Double-modifier key combos unlikely to collide with NT8 defaults.
⑥ Strategy/Indicator OnKeyDown
(companion approach)		 POSSIBLE NT8 Strategy/Indicator exposes OnKeyDown(KeyEventArgs) override. Fires when the chart has keyboard focus. Could write a "PTT Keyboard Relay" indicator that sits on the chart and sends signals to CopyEngine via a shared volatile flag or ConcurrentQueue. Architecturally clean — NT8 itself provides the routing. ⚠ CLEAN but breaks zero-config pillar. User must manually add indicator to every chart. Not suitable as primary mechanism — viable as opt-in enhancement.
⑦ Win32 SetWindowsHookEx
(global hook)		 BANNED System-wide keyboard hook via Win32 API. Would fire regardless of focus, regardless of NT8 interception. Requires P/Invoke into user32.dll. ❌ BANNED — NinjaScript sandbox restricts unmanaged P/Invoke. Also dangerous: intercepts keyboard system-wide including typing in other apps.
⑧ Mouse click — physical button
(current primary, confirmed)		 CONFIRMED All PTT action buttons (Trim, Flatten, Cancel, BE, Flatten ⚡) are physical WPF buttons in the ChartTrader panel row and AddOn window. Click = action fires. No focus dependency. No keyboard. Works unconditionally. ✅ CONFIRMED PRIMARY. Unconditional. Zero focus dependency. Already live and tested across B1–B9. Keyboard is Layer 2 enhancement only.
Architecture Decision — Keyboard Independence Principle (LOCKED)
Mouse click is the primary and reliable trigger for every PTT action. No PTT feature depends on keyboard input to function correctly. Every action is always reachable via a clearly labelled physical button in the panel. Keyboard shortcuts are a convenience enhancement layer only — if any shortcut fails to fire (for any reason), the button always works.

For the keyboard enhancement layer: Mechanism ⑤ (Chart Window PreviewKeyDown) is the best candidate. It fires regardless of which sub-element has focus within the chart window, requires no user configuration, and uses double-modifier combos (e.g. Ctrl+Shift+T) that are unlikely to collide with NT8's native shortcuts.

Mechanism ① (Shift+letter KeyBinding) is eliminated. All existing spec references to Shift+T / Shift+F / Shift+C / Shift+B as keyboard triggers are retracted pending mechanism ⑤ validation.
Open Investigation — 2 Sim101 Tests Required Before Locking Keyboard Layer
Test ID What to test Pass condition Gates
DW-B11-HK-01 Hook chart.PreviewKeyDown on Chart Window at injection time. Press Ctrl+Shift+T while chart has focus. Does handler fire? Handler fires with correct key combo. No crash. Cleans up on unhook. Mechanism ⑤ confirmation → unlock keyboard layer for all buttons
DW-B11-HK-02 With chart.PreviewKeyDown hooked: click chart canvas (moves focus away from our panel). Press Ctrl+Shift+T. Does handler still fire? Handler fires even when focus is on chart canvas, price axis, or any other chart element. Confirms focus-independence — the main advantage over KeyBinding
User tip (doc it in README): NT8 Tools → Keyboard → ChartTrader section lets users remap NT8's native "Close" action to any key. For a keyboard panic route using NT8's own system, map Ctrl+F = Close Position there. PTT does not conflict with NT8's native keyboard commands — our PreviewKeyDown handler only acts on its own designated combos and marks the event handled only for those.
B10 Implementation — Exact CopyEngine.cs Changes
Minimal change. Only Trim() and Flatten() in CopyEngine.cs are touched. New panic path added as a separate method. Zero changes to copy logic, bracket relay, or ATM dispatch.

CopyEngine.FlattenPanic() — new method
// NEW — called only by Flatten ⚡ panic button
// Uses NT8's own account.Flatten() — market, guaranteed exit, no price needed
internal void FlattenPanic(Instrument instrument)
{
    foreach (var acc in AllAccounts(instrument))
    {
        try
        {
            acc.Flatten(new[] { instrument });  // market, NT8 handles
            StatusUpdate?.Invoke(acc.Name + ": PANIC flatten");
        }
        catch (Exception ex)
        {
            StatusUpdate?.Invoke("PTT-Panic error: " + ex.Message);
        }
    }
}
NT8 Architecture — The Building We Build On
The Building Analogy
NinjaTrader 8 is the concrete frame and load-bearing walls of a skyscraper. We cannot move those walls — but we can run our own pipes, wires, and windows through the gaps NT8 left open for add-ons. Every architectural decision in PTT traces back to one question: "what did NT8 leave open, and what did it seal shut?" This section is the answer — drawn directly from NT8's decompiled source (NinjaTrader.Gui.dll + NinjaTrader.Core.dll, ILSpy decompile, 2025).
NT8 Internal Layer Stack (top → bottom)
Floor 5 — PTT ADD-ON LAYER (our code lives here)
TradeCopierPanel
UserControl in ChartTrader Grid row
TradeCopierAddOn
AddOnBase — injects panel, hooks events
CopyEngine
Singleton — rule table, order routing
AtrSizingEngine
Per-chart ATR qty calculator
↕ open seams: AddOnBase events + Account.CreateOrder + visual tree walk
Floor 4 — NT8 GUI LAYER (NinjaTrader.Gui.dll) — closed source, decompiled
ChartTrader
UserControl. Owns the Buy/Sell/Close buttons. Contains cbxAccounts : AccountSelector and private Account account (singular).
AccountSelector
extends ComboBox — single-select only. Exposes SelectedAccount : Account (one account). Internal ObservableCollection is the display list, NOT multi-selection.
Chart (Window)
Inherits System.Windows.Window. Contains ChartTrader as a child UserControl. ChartTrader.Content is a WPF Grid — our injection point.
↕ public API seam: Account.CreateOrder, Account.OrderUpdate, Account.All
Floor 3 — NT8 CBI LAYER (NinjaTrader.Core.dll) — closed source, decompiled
Account
static Collection<Account> All — every connected account. CreateOrder(...) — the ONE order submission method. event OrderUpdate — fires after broker accepts.
Order
Immutable after submit. Carries Instrument, Action, Qty, Price, OrderState. Read by CopyEngine on OrderUpdate.
Instrument
ChartTrader.Instrument — read via visual tree to identify what chart is showing.
AtmStrategy
NT8's bracket template engine. ChartTrader reads cbxStrategySelector. We dispatch per-follower ATM via FollowerAtmMode.
↕ sealed: broker socket, order engine internals, pre-submit hook
Floor 2 — Broker Adapter (NinjaTrader.Rithmic.dll / .IB.dll / .Tradovate.dll …) — fully sealed
One DLL per broker. Translates Account.CreateOrder into wire protocol. Not accessible from AddOn context.
Floor 1 — Broker / Exchange Network — fully sealed
Rithmic, Interactive Brokers, Tradovate, Schwab … Order hits exchange here.
Q: Can we make NT8's account dropdown multi-select and fire multiple CreateOrder calls natively?
SHORT ANSWER: No — and here is the decompiled proof.
1. AccountSelector is a ComboBox — single-select by design.
From NinjaTrader.Gui.Tools.AccountSelector (decompiled): 
public class AccountSelector : ComboBox, ICleanable
{
    // display list — NOT selection state
    private readonly ObservableCollection<Account> accounts;

    // ONE account property — singular
    public NinjaTrader.Cbi.Account SelectedAccount { get; set; }
}
2. ChartTrader stores one Account and SubmitOrder takes one Account.
From NinjaTrader.Gui.Chart.ChartTrader (decompiled): 
// private field — singular
private Account account;

// internal button handler routes here
public void OnSubmitOrder(object sender, RoutedEventArgs e) { }

// static method — takes ONE account, not a list
internal static Task SubmitOrder(
    Account account,        // <-- singular. always one.
    Instrument instrument,
    OrderAction action,
    ...
) { }
3. Even if we swapped the ComboBox visually, ChartTrader's button handlers read this.account (the private field) — not our widget.
Replacing the visual element changes nothing about the internal Account variable. The Buy/Sell button click chain is: btnBuyMkt.Click → OnSubmitOrder → SubmitOrder(this.account, ...) — fully obfuscated internal wiring we cannot inject into.
GOOD NEWS: Our architecture is already the correct and complete solution.
Your instinct is exactly right about the concept — we need N calls to Account.CreateOrder(), one per account. NT8 won't do that for us from its own dropdown. But CopyEngine already does it — and it does it better:
HYPOTHETICAL: "Multi-select NT8 dropdown"
NT8 fires CreateOrder on Account A
NT8 fires CreateOrder on Account B
NT8 fires CreateOrder on Account C

✗ Same qty on all accounts — no multipliers
✗ Same ATM template on all — no per-account ATM
✗ No mirror mode logic
✗ Not achievable — NT8 API is single-account
ACTUAL: CopyEngine (what we have)
Leader submits via ChartTrader (1 account)
Account.OrderUpdate fires
CopyEngine reads order parameters
follower.CreateOrder(...) × N

✓ Per-follower qty multiplier (B8)
✓ Per-follower ATM mode (B8)
✓ Mirror mode / Signal mode (B9)
✓ Works today with NT8 as-is

What NT8 Exposes vs Seals — Every Seam We Use
NT8 Feature Open / Sealed What It Gives Us PTT Use

Account.OrderUpdate (event) ✓ Open Fires on every order state change for that account. Our copy trigger — fires after leader order is accepted. CopyEngine: leaderAccount.OrderUpdate += OnLeaderOrderUpdate
Account.AccountItemUpdate ✓ Open Fires on P&L, margin, position changes per account. Used for live P&L display and pending BE price proxy (GAP-002). FollowerItem.OnAccountItemUpdate → UpdatePnl()

Account.Change(Order[]) ✓ Open Modifies working orders (stop price, limit price, qty). Used for BE and mirror stop relay. GAP-001d tests trail survival. CopyEngine.BreakEven, pending mirror stop relay (B10)

ChartTrader.SubmitOrder (static) ✗ Sealed (internal + obfuscated) Takes a single Account. Method body is obfuscated by AgileDotNet. Cannot be called or injected into from AddOn context. Not accessible

B11 — Keyboard Shortcut Architecture (Layer 2)
⚠ KEY FINDING: Shift+letter KeyBinding is CONFIRMED BROKEN at NT8 runtime
ARCHITECTURE CONSTRAINT — APPLIES TO ALL KEYBOARD SHORTCUTS IN THIS SPEC
All Shift+T, Shift+F, Shift+C, Shift+B shortcuts listed elsewhere in this spec are aspirational only. They are documented as the intended design but do not fire at runtime.

Root cause: NT8 intercepts all Shift+letter key combinations at the application level before they reach the AddOn's WPF KeyBinding handlers. Adding a WPF KeyBinding(Key.T, ModifierKeys.Shift) to the injected panel compiles without errors but the callback never fires — NT8 silently swallows the key. This is the same category as NT8-003 (volatile double banned) — a runtime behaviour that has no compile-time signal.
Keyboard mechanismStatusNotes
WPF KeyBinding (Shift+letter) on injected panel ✗ BROKEN at runtime NT8 intercepts Shift+letter globally. Compiles fine. Never fires. All Shift+T/F/C/B in this spec are aspirational only.
WPF KeyBinding (plain letter) on injected panel ◯ Unknown — requires Sim101 test Plain letter keys (T, F, C, B without modifier) may be free. Focus-dependent — only fires if the panel has WPF keyboard focus.
chart.PreviewKeyDown — Chart Window level ✓ Best candidate — fires focus-independent Chart-level keyboard event hook. Fires regardless of which WPF element has focus inside the chart window. Can intercept Ctrl+Shift combinations before NT8 UI processes them.
Ctrl+Shift+letter via PreviewKeyDown ◯ Pending DW-B11-HK-01 Sim101 test Three-key combinations (Ctrl+Shift+T) are less likely to conflict with NT8 built-in shortcuts. DW-B11-HK-01 must confirm this fires at all.
Mouse click (primary interaction) ✓ UNCONDITIONAL — always works Mouse click on injected buttons = primary interaction. Layer 1. No NT8 interference. Architecture decision locked: mouse click is unconditional, keyboard is Layer 2 enhancement only.
Shift+letter CONFIRMED BROKEN — NT8 intercepts at app level ✓ Mouse click = primary (unconditional) chart.PreviewKeyDown = best keyboard candidate Keyboard = Layer 2 enhancement only — never required
B11 — Keyboard Layer 2 Design (chart.PreviewKeyDown)
If Sim101 tests confirm chart.PreviewKeyDown fires for Ctrl+Shift+T/F/C/B, keyboard shortcuts are wired at the Chart Window level — not on the injected panel. The handler calls the exact same CopyEngine methods that the buttons call. No separate code paths. No state owned by the keyboard handler.
Proposed shortcut map (all pending Sim101 confirmation)
Shortcut (proposed) Action CopyEngine call Status
Ctrl+Shift+T Trim ½ — all follower accounts CopyEngine.Trim(rule) ◯ DW-B11-HK-01 pending
Ctrl+Shift+F Flatten all — all follower accounts CopyEngine.FlattenAll(rule) ◯ DW-B11-HK-01 pending
Ctrl+Shift+C Cancel all pending entries — all follower accounts CopyEngine.CancelPendingEntries(rule) ◯ DW-B11-HK-01 pending
Ctrl+Shift+B Move all stops to break-even CopyEngine.MoveStopToBreakEven(rule) ◯ DW-B11-HK-01 pending 
// chart.PreviewKeyDown hook — wired in DoInject() on dispatcher thread
// CYC=5: four Ctrl+Shift key checks + default fallthrough
// PreviewKeyDown fires BEFORE WPF routing — focus-independent within the chart window
private void OnChartPreviewKeyDown(object sender, KeyEventArgs e)
{
    if ((Keyboard.Modifiers & (ModifierKeys.Control | ModifierKeys.Shift))
        != (ModifierKeys.Control | ModifierKeys.Shift)) return;  // guard: Ctrl+Shift required

    var rule = _rule;  // snapshot — avoid race with UI thread
    if (rule == null) return;

    switch (e.Key)
    {
        case Key.T: CopyEngine.Instance?.Trim(rule);                  e.Handled = true; break;
        case Key.F: CopyEngine.Instance?.FlattenAll(rule);            e.Handled = true; break;
        case Key.C: CopyEngine.Instance?.CancelPendingEntries(rule);  e.Handled = true; break;
        case Key.B: CopyEngine.Instance?.MoveStopToBreakEven(rule);   e.Handled = true; break;
    }
}

// Wire in DoInject() after panel is attached — unhook in OnDestroyed()
chart.PreviewKeyDown += OnChartPreviewKeyDown;   // ← subscribe
// OnDestroyed: chart.PreviewKeyDown -= OnChartPreviewKeyDown;  ← required (leak guard)

B11 — ATR Engine Full Architecture
Q: Contract Rounding Policy — ✓ LOCKED: Ceil
When maxRisk / riskPerCont is not a whole number, three policies were evaluated. MES parameters used for all calculations: tick = $1.25 · ticksPerPoint = 4 · pointDollarValue = $5 · maxRisk = $200 · atrFraction = 1.0. Stop distance always rounded up first (conservative ceil on point boundary), so riskPerCont is always whole.

Max overshoot: +$40 (ATR=12, 1 extra contract)
Max undershoot: none
Odd qty at key ATR levels (7pts → 6 contracts)

✓ maxRisk is a budget ceiling — never violate it from below
✓ Odd qty fine — Trim() uses ceil(n/2), cleans up correctly
✓ Overshoot bounded: worst case +1 contract × stop distance
FLOOR QTY — rejected
Max overshoot: none
Max undershoot: -$35 (ATR=11)
Always ≤ maxRisk

✗ Systematically under-sizes — always leaves money on the table
✗ At ATR=6–7 drops from 7→6 contracts (- $30 exposure)
EVEN-FLOOR QTY — rejected
Max overshoot: none
Max undershoot: -$90 (ATR=11 → drops 3→2)
Always even, half-trim always clean

✗ Worst deviation of the three options
✗ Trim benefit marginal — existing Trim() ceil(n/2) handles odd qty correctly already
✗ Extra contract lost for cosmetic convenience
▼ DECISION LOCKED — Ceil qty + Ceil stop (Two-Ceiling Formula)
maxRisk is a risk budget, not a soft target. The user's intent is "never risk more than $200 on a stop-out per trade" — ceil enforces this from the stop-distance side (never a shorter stop than ATR demands) while potentially adding 1 contract. The worst-case overshoot across the entire MES ATR range (4–12 pts) is +$40 at ATR=12 — one extra contract at the widest stop. At ATR=7 (the most common live MES level), ceil produces 6 contracts × $35/contract = $210 — an acceptable +$10 over target. The "even qty makes trimming easier" argument is rejected: Trim() already uses ceil(n/2) and handles 5-contract positions correctly (trims 3, keeps 2 — which is what you'd want anyway).
✓ Two-Ceiling: ceil(stop) + ceil(qty) Max overshoot: +$40 at ATR=12 Trim() ceil(n/2) handles odd qty correctly Locked B11-session-2026-07-09
B11 — Bar Series Auto-Detect (BarsArray[0].BarsPeriod)
The current AtrSizingEngine.cs hardcodes AddDataSeries(Minute, 1) in OnStateChange.Configure. This means the engine always attaches a 1-minute data series regardless of which chart it is placed on. The user trades a 5-minute chart, so ATR should be calculated on 5-minute bars. Hard-coding 1-minute is wrong — a 1-min ATR is much more volatile than a 5-min ATR, and if the chart type changes (e.g. 3-min, Renko), the sizing engine diverges from the chart's displayed ATR.

Fix: In OnStateChange.Configure, call AddDataSeries(BarsArray[0].BarsPeriod) to mirror the primary series. NT8 Indicator API: AddDataSeries(BarsPeriodType type, int value) or the overload accepting a BarsPeriod struct directly. The primary chart's period is available as BarsArray[0].BarsPeriod in the Configure state. 
// CURRENT (hardcoded 1-min — WRONG for 5-min chart):
else if (State == NinjaTrader.NinjaScript.State.Configure)
{
    AddDataSeries(NinjaTrader.Data.BarsPeriodType.Minute, 1);  // ← hardcoded
}

// TARGET (auto-detect from chart's primary series):
else if (State == NinjaTrader.NinjaScript.State.Configure)
{
    // Mirror BarsArray[0] — whatever period the chart is using
    AddDataSeries(BarsArray[0].BarsPeriod.BarsPeriodType,
                  BarsArray[0].BarsPeriod.Value);
}
// Src change: AtrSizingEngine.cs::OnStateChange — staged for ptt-orchestrator. NOT a direct .cs edit.
NT8 compiler note: BarsArray[0].BarsPeriod is only valid in State.Configure or later — not in State.SetDefaults (no data series attached yet). This placement is correct. The extra data series created by AddDataSeries becomes BarsArray[1] but the ATR calculation in OnBarUpdate calls ATR(Period)[0] which references the primary series — correct, no change needed in OnBarUpdate.
◯ Pending ptt-orchestrator workflow Scope: AtrSizingEngine.cs OnStateChange.Configure only CYC unchanged (4 branches, same structure)
B11 — Two-Ceiling Formula (atrFraction + ceil qty) — CalcContracts Upgrade
The current formula (Math.Floor, no atrFraction) is a clean baseline. The upgrade introduces two changes: (1) an atrFraction multiplier so the user can set stop distance as a fraction of ATR (e.g. 0.75× for a tighter stop), and (2) switch qty rounding from floor to ceil per the locked decision above. Stop distance is always ceil'd to a whole point first (conservative — never a shorter stop than ATR demands). 
// CURRENT CalcContracts — Math.Floor, no fraction (AtrSizingEngine.cs line 95)
internal static int CalcContracts(double atrPoints, double maxRisk, double tickDollarValue)
{
    if (atrPoints       <= 0) return 1;
    if (tickDollarValue <= 0) return 1;
    double riskPerContract = atrPoints * tickDollarValue;
    int contracts = (int)Math.Floor(maxRisk / riskPerContract);
    return contracts < 1 ? 1 : contracts;
}

// TARGET CalcContracts — Two-Ceiling formula, atrFraction param
// Note: tickDollarValue is actually pointDollarValue in current code (e.g. $5 for MES).
// atrFraction default = 1.0 (full ATR stop). Use 0.75 for tighter stops, 1.25 for wider.
// CYC stays at 3 — same guard structure, only math inside changes.
internal static int CalcContracts(double atrPoints, double maxRisk,
                                    double pointDollarValue, double atrFraction = 1.0)
{
    if (atrPoints        <= 0) return 1;   // guard (1)
    if (pointDollarValue <= 0) return 1;   // guard (2)

    // Ceil 1: whole-point stop distance (conservative — never shorter than ATR fraction)
    double stopPoints  = Math.Ceiling(atrPoints * atrFraction);

    // Ceil 2: qty — never under-sizes the risk budget
    double riskPerCont = stopPoints * pointDollarValue;
    int    contracts   = (int)Math.Ceiling(maxRisk / riskPerCont);

    return contracts < 1 ? 1 : contracts;  // guard (3): clamp minimum to 1
}

// Callers update: SetParameters gains atrFraction param. OnBarUpdate passes it through.
// Staged for ptt-orchestrator. Do NOT edit .cs directly.
Worked examples — MES, maxRisk=$200, pointDollarValue=$5, atrFraction=1.0
ATR (pts)Template equivalentstopPoints = ceil(ATR)riskPerContqty = ceil(200÷risk)Actual exposure
4RMA 4SL Q104 pts (16t)$2010$200 (±0)
5RMA 5SL Q105 pts (20t)$258$200 (±0)
6RMA 6SL Q86 pts (24t)$307$210 (+$10)
7RMA 7SL Q67 pts (28t)$356$210 (+$10)
8RMA 8SL Q68 pts (32t)$405$200 (±0)
Template comparison note: Your existing MES RMA templates use EntryQty of 10, 10, 8, 6, 6 for ATR 4–8. The two-ceiling formula yields 10, 8, 7, 6, 5. Differences: ATR=5 (formula 8 vs template 10 — template was over-sized), ATR=6 (formula 7 vs template 8 — close), ATR=8 (formula 5 vs template 6 — formula is tighter). The formula-driven qty is more mathematically consistent than the hand-tuned templates.

B11 — ATM Template Sizing Path 2: Dynamic Template XML Write
Goal: Before each trade, write a fresh ATM template XML file with the ATR-computed stop distance (in ticks) and entry quantity, then select that template in ChartTrader. NT8 reads the ATM template file at the moment the user selects it from the dropdown (or when the dropdown is programmatically set) — not at compile time — so writing the file just before triggering the dropdown selection is sufficient to inject new values.

Why Path 2 over re-using static templates: The 5 existing MES RMA templates cover ATR 4–8 pts at round integers only. The ATR formula produces fractional ATR values (e.g. 6.3 pts → stopPts=7 → 6 contracts) that don't map cleanly to any single template. A dynamically-written template handles all ATR values precisely.
Decision pointAnswerRationale
Template file location %USERPROFILE%\Documents\NinjaTrader 8\templates\AtmStrategy\PTT_ATR_LIVE.xml Fixed filename — same path every time. NT8 scans this directory on dropdown open. Overwriting in-place is safe.
Which XML fields to write StopLoss ticks + EntryQuantity only All other fields (trail, target=8t, auto-BE, StopTargetHandling, CalculationMode) are identical across all 5 RMA templates — copy from any static template as the base and overwrite only these two.
When to write Immediately before the user (or ATR engine) selects the template NT8 reads template XML when selected — write must complete before selection fires. In practice: write in ApplyResize() after CalcContracts, before SetAtmTemplate call.
NT8 dropdown sync (Sim101 pending) Set ChartTrader's strategy name ComboBox value to "PTT_ATR_LIVE" If NT8 reads the template at selection time (not at startup), Path 2 works standalone. The pending Sim101 test confirms this. If NT8 caches at startup, Path 3 (UI write) is required as a layer on top.
Thread safety Write on dispatcher thread (UI thread) ApplyResize() is called from Panel UI context. File.WriteAllText is synchronous — acceptable for a one-time per-bar-close write to a small XML file.
Atomicity Write to temp file → File.Move (overwrite) Prevents NT8 reading a partial file mid-write. Temp file in same directory ensures same-drive atomic rename. 
// New: AtrTemplateWriter.cs (static helper — no NT8 base class needed)
// CYC=3: guard (stopTicks≤0) + File.Move atomic write + error-catch
internal static class AtrTemplateWriter
{
    private static readonly string TemplateName = "PTT_ATR_LIVE";
    private static readonly string TemplateDir  =
        System.IO.Path.Combine(
            Environment.GetFolderPath(Environment.SpecialFolder.MyDocuments),
            @"NinjaTrader 8\templates\AtmStrategy");

    // Write dynamic template. stopTicks = ceil(ATR*fraction) × ticksPerPoint.
    // entryCQty from CalcContracts(). Both brackets set to qty=1 per PerEntryExecution.
    internal static void Write(int stopTicks, int entryQty)
    {
        if (stopTicks <= 0 || entryQty <= 0) return;  // guard
        string xml  = BuildXml(stopTicks, entryQty);
        string dest = System.IO.Path.Combine(TemplateDir, TemplateName + ".xml");
        string tmp  = dest + ".tmp";
        System.IO.File.WriteAllText(tmp, xml, System.Text.Encoding.UTF8);
        System.IO.File.Move(tmp, dest, overwrite: true);  // atomic rename
    }

    // Pure XML builder — no NT8 dependencies. CYC=1. Testable without NT8.
    private static string BuildXml(int stopTicks, int entryQty)
    {
        // Base: MES RMA 6SL Q8 structure (all shared fields identical across templates)
        // Only StopLoss ticks and EntryQuantity overridden per computed values.
        // StopTargetHandling=PerEntryExecution, CalculationMode=Ticks, Target=8t
        // Bracket1 qty=1, Bracket2 qty=1 (PerEntryExecution splits evenly)
        return $@"<?xml version=""1.0"" encoding=""utf-8""?>
<AtmStrategy>
  <Name>{TemplateName}</Name>
  <StopTargetHandling>PerEntryExecution</StopTargetHandling>
  <CalculationMode>Ticks</CalculationMode>
  <EntryQuantity>{entryQty}</EntryQuantity>
  <Brackets>
    <AtmStrategyBracket>
      <Quantity>1</Quantity>
      <Target>8</Target>
      <StopLoss>{stopTicks}</StopLoss>
      <TrailingStop Name=""MES 8sl 2_trail"" />
    </AtmStrategyBracket>
    <AtmStrategyBracket>
      <Quantity>1</Quantity>
      <Target>8</Target>
      <StopLoss>{stopTicks}</StopLoss>
      <TrailingStop Name=""MES 8sl 2_trail"" />
    </AtmStrategyBracket>
  </Brackets>
</AtmStrategy>";
    }
}

B11 — SyncAtrStops() Full Wiring Spec
When ATR changes on bar close, existing Working (fixed) stop orders on both leader and follower accounts are updated to the new ATR-derived stop distance via acc.Change(). This keeps live stops calibrated to current volatility without any manual intervention.
Call flow — bar close to stop update
NT8 bar close
AtrSizingEngine
.OnBarUpdate()
→
sizing + template

AtrTemplateWriter.Write()
→
stop sync
(if enabled)

.SyncAtrStops()
→
per account
ApplyAtrStop
ToAccount()
→
NT8 API

order[])
// ── CopyEngine.SyncAtrStops() ─────────────────────────────────────────────
// Called from AtrSizingEngine.OnBarUpdate() after CalcContracts + template write.
// CYC outer = 5: guard (rule null) + guard (not enabled) + guard (flat) +
//   account loop (body extracted into ApplyAtrStopToAccount, CYC=4)
internal void SyncAtrStops(CopyRule rule, Instrument instrument, double newAtrPoints)
{
    if (rule == null)                    return;  // guard (1)
    if (!rule.EnableAtrStopSync)         return;  // guard (2) feature flag

    double stopDist = Math.Ceiling(newAtrPoints * rule.AtrStopFraction)
                       * instrument.MasterInstrument.TickSize;

    foreach (var acc in AllAccounts(rule))        // leader + all follower accounts
        ApplyAtrStopToAccount(acc, instrument, stopDist, rule.AtrStopSyncMinTicks,
                               instrument.MasterInstrument.TickSize);
}

// ── CopyEngine.ApplyAtrStopToAccount() ───────────────────────────────────
// CYC=4: no-position guard + order loop (trail skip + no-change guard + safety guard)
private static void ApplyAtrStopToAccount(
    Account acc, Instrument instr,
    double stopDist, int minTicks, double tickSize)
{
    var pos = acc.Positions.FindByInstrument(instr);
    if (pos == null || pos.Quantity == 0)  return;  // guard: flat

    bool isLong = pos.MarketPosition == MarketPosition.Long;

    foreach (var order in acc.Orders.FindByInstrument(instr))
    {
        if (order.OrderState != OrderState.Working)      continue;
        if (order.OrderType != OrderType.StopMarket
         && order.OrderType != OrderType.StopLimit)      continue;
        if (order.TrailPrice > 0)                        continue;  // skip trailing

        double newStop = isLong
            ? pos.AveragePrice - stopDist
            : pos.AveragePrice + stopDist;

        double delta = Math.Abs(newStop - order.StopPrice);
        if (delta < minTicks * tickSize)                  continue;  // noise filter

        // Price safety: never move stop so far it would immediately trigger
        double bid = instr.MarketData.Bid.Price;
        double ask = instr.MarketData.Ask.Price;
        bool wouldTrigger = isLong ? newStop >= bid : newStop <= ask;
        if (wouldTrigger)                                  continue;

        order.StopPrice = newStop;
        acc.Change(new Order[] { order });
    }
}

// ── OnBarUpdate trigger (AtrSizingEngine.cs) ─────────────────────────────
// After CalcContracts + AtrTemplateWriter.Write() call:
CopyEngine.Instance?.SyncAtrStops(_rule, Instrument, _lastAtr);
Skip conditions — SyncAtrStops will NOT modify an order if:
✗ EnableAtrStopSync = false (per-rule flag)
✗ Account is flat for this instrument
✗ Order state ≠ Working
✗ Order type is not StopMarket or StopLimit
✗ Trailing stop (TrailPrice > 0)
✗ Price delta < AtrStopSyncMinTicks (noise filter)
✗ New stop would immediately trigger (safety guard)
✗ Profit target orders (Limit type — never touched)

✓ RESOLVED — B11-T6: SyncPendingEntry() — Architecture locked: cancel+resubmit (Option B)
Resolution source: NT8-K-006 confirmed (7/10/2026 log, 3 sessions, 9 test sequences). ChartTrader's increase logic spawns delta orders — follower accounts may have an order stack. Account.Change() on a single order cannot fix total qty across a stack. Option B (cancel all pending entries + resubmit single fresh order) is the only correct approach. CancelPendingEntries() already implemented. See NT8-K-006 card below for full evidence.
SCOPE RULE — SyncPendingEntry() only touches FOLLOWER accounts. Leader is never modified by the copier.
ScenarioLeader ordersFollower ordersWho acts
ATR shifts on bar close — pending entry exists Your orders, your ChartTrader stack. Copier never touches them. Copier calls CancelPendingEntries(followerAcc, instr) on each follower, then CreateOrder(newQty). Copier acts on followers only
Leader cancels manually (already implemented B8/B9) You cancel via ChartTrader. OnOrderUpdate(Cancelled) fires → copier cancels all follower copies. Copier mirrors cancel to followers
Follower pending entry before ATR shift Not involved. Single order placed by copier via acc.CreateOrder() — no ChartTrader delta-stack, always exactly 1 order per follower. Cancel 1 order + resubmit 1 order
Key insight: Follower accounts never accumulate a ChartTrader delta-order stack because the copier places entries via acc.CreateOrder() directly — bypassing ChartTrader entirely. So each follower always has exactly 1 pending entry order at any time. CancelPendingEntries() cancels that 1 order. CreateOrder(newQty) places 1 fresh order. No stack management needed on followers — the complexity seen in the ChartTrader tests only applies to the leader's own account.
Background — why this question arose: When the bar closes before the leader's entry fills, ATR may shift (e.g. 4pts → 5pts), changing the correct qty (e.g. 10 → 8 contracts). The ATM template was already attached at submission time with the old qty. SyncAtrStops() skips the pending bracket (it is in Accepted state, not Working). The follower's copied entry is in Working state with the wrong qty. Cancel+resubmit on followers is the correct fix.

Cancel + resubmit as fallback if Change() on entry qty is unsupported CopyEngine.CancelPendingEntries() already exists — proven API ✓ Fallback available
Option A — Modify in place (Account.Change)
// On bar close: find Working entry, update qty
var entry = FindWorkingEntry(acc, instr);
if (entry != null && entry.Quantity != newQty)
{
    entry.Quantity = newQty;
    acc.Change(new Order[] { entry });
}
✓ Same order ID — no gap at broker
✓ ATM re-evaluates with new qty on fill
✗ UNVERIFIED — broker may reject qty change on working limit
Option B — Cancel + resubmit
// On bar close: if qty changed, cancel + new order
var entry = FindWorkingEntry(acc, instr);
if (entry != null && entry.Quantity != newQty)
{
    acc.Cancel(new Order[] { entry });
    // resubmit with newQty at same limit price
    acc.CreateOrder(instr, entry.OrderAction,
        entry.OrderType, ..., newQty,
        entry.LimitPrice, ...);
}
✓ Works on all brokers — standard cancel/replace
✓ CancelPendingEntries() already proven
✗ Brief gap: order cancelled before new one confirmed
✗ New order ID — dedup cache must handle it

▶ ATR Feature — Architectural Risk Assessment: ON/OFF reliability, code isolation, copy path impact
Questions answered before committing to B11 execution: Does OFF mean truly off? Does ATR add complexity to the copy path? How much code? Does it affect copy reliability?
✓ Hard gate — EnableAtrSizing = false means zero ATR execution
OnBarUpdate() fires on every bar close.
First line: if (!rule.EnableAtrSizing) return;
All ATR methods — CalcContracts(), AtrTemplateWriter.Write(), SyncAtrStops(), SyncPendingEntry() — are never called.
No state is accumulated. No orders touched. No template files written.
⚠ One unavoidable footprint when OFF
AtrSizingEngine.SetParameters() calls AddDataSeries() at indicator load time — before the ON/OFF flag is read.
The data series subscription exists regardless of the flag.
Impact: microseconds of extra bar processing overhead. Zero effect on order reliability.
This is a known NT8 architectural constraint — data series subscriptions cannot be conditional.
Does ATR add complexity to always-on code paths?

AtrTemplateWriter.Write() — XML write New static class, CYC=3+1 Never called by copy path Isolated — pure ATR
SyncAtrStops() — live stop sync New method, CYC=5+4 Runs on bar close, not on fill Isolated — different cadence
SyncPendingEntry() — entry resize New method, CYC=3 Calls CancelPendingEntries() (shared) ⚠ One edge case — see below

⚠ The one genuine interaction risk — SyncPendingEntry() vs OnExecutionUpdate() race
Scenario: Bar closes at the exact same moment the leader's entry fills. OnBarUpdate() fires → SyncPendingEntry() tries to cancel follower entries. Simultaneously, OnExecutionUpdate() fires → copy path detects leader fill → tries to mirror the fill to followers.

NT8 dispatcher behaviour: NT8 dispatches all callbacks sequentially on the UI dispatcher — they cannot truly run simultaneously. One will complete before the other starts. But the order of dispatch is not guaranteed.

Required guard in SyncPendingEntry(): if (leaderHasOpenPosition(instr)) return; // don't cancel follower entries if leader just filled This guard makes the race benign: if the leader filled, SyncPendingEntry() exits early. If SyncPendingEntry() ran first and cancelled the follower entry, the copy path detects leader fill and places a fresh market/limit order for the follower anyway. Either order of execution produces a correct result.
OFF = truly off
One data series overhead only. Zero order/copy impact.
Copy path untouched
Zero new branches in OnExecutionUpdate() or HandleBracketChange().

All new methods appended. No existing method edits. All CYC ≤ 8.

NT8-K-006 CONFIRMED — ChartTrader qty management is ASYMMETRIC (3 sessions, 5 test sequences)
SessionActionBeforeAfterNT8 responseOrder IDs
9:48 (S1) ▼ Decrease 10→9 78907a Qty=10 78907a Qty=9 Change submitted on same ID 1 order
9:48 (S1) ▲ Increase 9→12 78907a Qty=9 78907a Qty=9 + e0bdec Qty=3 New order e0bdec for delta=3 2 orders
10:01 (S2) ▲ Increase 10→13 8cce47 Qty=10 8cce47 Qty=10 + cbdf38 Qty=3 New order cbdf38 for delta=3 2 orders
10:01 (S2) ▼ Decrease 13→11 8cce47 Qty=10 + cbdf38 Qty=3 8cce47 Qty=10 + cbdf38 Qty=1 Change submitted on delta order cbdf38 (3→1). Original 8cce47 untouched. 2 orders (delta shrinks)
10:01 (S2) ▼ Decrease 11→8 8cce47 Qty=10 + cbdf38 Qty=1 8cce47 Qty=8 (cbdf38 cancelled) cbdf38 cancelled, 8cce47 modified 10→8. Decrease consumed delta order first, then modified original. 1 order (delta eliminated)
10:01 (S2) ▼ Decrease 8→6 8cce47 Qty=8 8cce47 Qty=6 Change submitted on 8cce47 (8→6) 1 order
10:01 (S2) ▲ Increase 6→10 8cce47 Qty=6 8cce47 Qty=6 + b3357c Qty=4 New order b3357c for delta=4 2 orders
10:08 (S3) ▲ Increase 8→10 f05032 Qty=8 f05032 Qty=8 + 71dae2 Qty=2 New order 71dae2 for delta=2 2 orders
10:08 (S3) ▲ Increase 10→13 f05032 Qty=8 + 71dae2 Qty=2 f05032 Qty=8 + 71dae2 Qty=2 + 6d9189 Qty=3 New order 6d9189 for delta=3. Stack grows. 3 orders
Complete ChartTrader qty management rules (all confirmed)
▼ Decrease rules
1. If a delta order exists: reduce it first (Change submitted)
2. If delta order would go to 0: cancel it, then modify original
3. If no delta order: modify original directly
4. The original order is touched last (only if delta exhausted)
→ Always results in fewer or equal order IDs
▲ Increase rules
1. Always creates a new order for the delta (newTotal − currentTotal)
2. Original order is never modified on increase
3. Each increase adds another order to the stack
4. Stack can grow to N orders (1 original + N−1 increases)
→ Always results in more order IDs
Root cause: This is 100% ChartTrader UI-layer logic. The broker API (Account.Change()) only operates on individual orders. ChartTrader manages the aggregate qty view and decides which individual orders to modify or create. The NT8 engine itself has no concept of "total position qty across multiple pending orders."
✓ ARCHITECTURE DECISION LOCKED — SyncPendingEntry() must use cancel+resubmit (Option B)
Why Option A (Account.Change on single order) cannot work for the copier:
The copier may have multiple pending entry orders on a follower account (original + delta stack from prior ATR increases). Calling Account.Change() on just one of them does not change the total qty the follower is working. The copier would need to replicate ChartTrader's full stack management logic — tracking which orders are "original" vs "delta", managing partial reductions across the stack, etc. That is ChartTrader's UI logic, not appropriate for the copier.

Option B (cancel + resubmit) is the correct architecture:
  1. On bar close, if ATR qty has changed: call CancelPendingEntries(acc, instr) — cancels ALL working entry orders for that instrument/account in one call (already implemented)
  2. Immediately resubmit a single fresh order at newQty via acc.CreateOrder() with the updated ATM template
  3. Result: exactly 1 clean order at the correct qty, correct ATM template, no stack complexity
The brief gap (order cancelled → new order accepted, ~0.5s on Rithmic sim) is acceptable. ATR only changes on bar close (max once per 5 min on 5-min chart). The price risk during the gap is negligible.
◯ NT8-K-005 still open — ATM bracket qty after entry qty change
After a resubmit with the new qty + updated ATM template XML (Path 2 rewrite): does the ATM bracket spawn at correct qty when filled? The resubmit path writes a fresh XML before CreateOrder() so the template is always current at submission time. This is lower-risk than the Change() path — but still needs a Sim101 fill test to confirm the bracket legs match the new template. Test: DW-B11-SIM-K005-01 — resubmit with modified template, let fill, verify bracket stop qty = template EntryQuantity.

B12 — Live-Adjustable Buffered Buttons (DW-B12-BUFFERED-BUTTONS-01)
Four action buttons carry a live tick-offset buffer — Trim, Flatten, and BE — each displays its current buffer number directly in the button label. Each button owns its own independent ▲▼ pair on the right edge. Three integer fields. Three ▲▼ pairs. No dialog. No settings menu. All limit orders — no market orders anywhere in the panel. Inspired by Affordable Indicators' static labels — but ours are editable in-place and limit-order native.
Buy Ask / Sell Bid entry buttons shelved → B13 (limit-order entries, pending trading workflow validation).
Affordable Indicators — static labels
"BE +1", "Bid +1", "Ask -1" are hardcoded per the product version.
To change the offset: open settings, edit a field, close.
Cannot adjust during live trading without interruption.
Buttons are full-width with no in-button controls.
✓ PTT — live-editable spinners in button
Label is the live value: clicking ▲ on "BE +1" changes it to "BE +2" instantly.
Left click the button body → execute action at current buffer.
Right edge: stacked ▲ / ▼ repeat-buttons (NT8 RepeatButton).
Zero dialogs. Zero interruption. Exactly like NT8's own qty spinner.
WPF composite button anatomy — locked 3-row layout

Flatten +1 ▲ ▼
BE +1 ▲ ▼

● COPY ON/OFF
Left zone (75%): click = execute  |  Right zone (25%): ▲▼ RepeatButtons

  └─ Col 1: 20px (arrow area)
